, and almost complex structure
\hat{J}
by construction.
15
15

15

This shows that we need to set up the moduli spaces and their description with a
t
-independent almost complex structure on the shrinking strip, and need to work with
t
-independent connections and exponential map on this strip.

For the section
\hat{\xi}
this is where we crucially use the restriction of the pointwise convergence requirement to “solutions modulo
\mathfrak{C}
” and the fact that we represented the cokernel in the form
(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)
. So the second component of the equation
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})=(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)\in\mathfrak{C}
becomes
0=\hat{\Phi}(\hat{\xi})^{-1}(\partial_{t}\hat{e}(\hat{\xi}))=\nabla_{t}\xi
,
which guarantees the desired pointwise convergence.

Pointwise
\mathcal{C}^{1}
-Continuity in
\mathbf{\Delta}
at Solutions Modulo
\mathbf{\mathfrak{C}}
:

By the previous item and Remark
2.12
it remains to consider the differentials at
solutions
(\gamma_{02},\hat{\gamma})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
of
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{02},\hat{\gamma})\in\mathfrak{C}
, in particular in case
\epsilon_{0}=0
this ensures that the section
\hat{\gamma}
is independent of
t\in[0,1]
.
Moreover, the only dependence on
\epsilon
in the differentials comes from linearizing

\hat{\xi}\mapsto\epsilon\hat{\Phi}(\hat{\xi})^{-1}(\hat{J}\partial_{s})\,\hat{e}(\hat{\xi})=:\epsilon\,\mathcal{H}(\hat{\xi})
, where we denote
\mathcal{H}(\hat{\xi}):=\hat{\Phi}(\hat{\xi})^{-1}(\hat{J}\partial_{s})\,\hat{e}(\hat{\xi})
.
Now given any
\epsilon_{0}\in[0,1]
and a solution

(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\in\Gamma
of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{02},\hat{\gamma})(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\in\mathfrak{C}
we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{02},\hat{\gamma})(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{02},\hat{\gamma})(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\bigl(\;0\;,\;(\epsilon-\epsilon_{0}){\rm D}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}\;\bigr)\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=|\epsilon-\epsilon_{0}|\bigl(\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl(\|{\rm D}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}+\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl({\rm D}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)+\epsilon^{-3/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl({\rm D}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}\bigr)\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-3/4}\|{\rm D}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{4}}\bigr)

which as before converges to
0
when
\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}>0
. For
\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}=0
the convergence requires

\nabla_{t}\bigl({\rm D}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}=0
, which is guaranteed by working at a solution modulo
\mathfrak{C}
.
Indeed,

{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(\gamma_{02},\hat{\gamma})(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})=(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)
implies

{\rm D}\mathcal{K}(\hat{\gamma})\hat{\xi}=0
for the linearization of
\mathcal{K}(\hat{\xi}):=\hat{\Phi}(\hat{\xi})^{-1}\partial_{t}\,\hat{e}(\hat{\xi})
, which amounts to the equation
\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}=0
.

Uniform Continuity of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
:

Generalizing the computations for
[
WW12
, Lemma 3.1.5]
to a second nonzero base point
(\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}})\neq(0,0)
proves

\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}})(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}})(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\leq c(\|(\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}})-(\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}})\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})\|(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

for all
(\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}}),(\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{02},\hat{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
,
(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\in\Gamma
, and
\epsilon\in[0,1]
with a linear function
c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(x)=C_{2}x
.

Uniform Bound on
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(0)
:

For
0<\epsilon\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\xi\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\bigl\|\bigl(\;\nabla_{t}\xi_{02}-J_{02}(u_{02})\nabla_{s}\xi_{02}-\nabla_{\xi_{02}}J_{02}\,\partial_{s}u_{02}\;,\;\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\hat{J}(\hat{u})\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\nabla_{\hat{\xi}}\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\;\bigr)\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}.

This is bounded by the sum of the two components: From the first component we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\xi_{02}\|_{H^{2}}\leq 1}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\xi_{02}-J_{02}(u_{02})\nabla_{s}\xi_{02}-\nabla_{\xi_{02}}J_{02}\,\partial_{s}u_{02}\bigr\|_{H^{1}}

which is independent of
\epsilon
.
From the second component we obtain the supremum over

\displaystyle 1\geq\|\hat{\xi}\|_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\,:=\;\epsilon^{1/2}\bigl(\|\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{s}^{2}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)+\epsilon^{1/4}\bigl(\|\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{4}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl(\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{t}\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{s}\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)+\epsilon^{-3/2}\|\nabla_{t}^{2}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-3/4}\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\|_{L^{4}}

of the terms arising from the second component of the
\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
norm,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\hat{\xi}\|_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\Bigl(\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\hat{J}(\hat{u})\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\nabla_{\hat{\xi}}\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\hat{J}(\hat{u})\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\nabla_{\hat{\xi}}\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt+\epsilon^{-3/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\hat{J}(\hat{u})\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\nabla_{\hat{\xi}}\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-3/4}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\hat{J}(\hat{u})\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}-\epsilon\,\nabla_{\hat{\xi}}\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\|\hat{\xi}\|_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\Bigl(\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl(\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\,\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\,\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl(\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla_{s}^{2}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\nabla\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-3/2}\bigl(\bigl\|\nabla_{t}^{2}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\nabla\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-3/4}\bigl(\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}+\epsilon\,\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}+\epsilon\,\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\|\hat{\xi}\|_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\Bigl(\epsilon^{-1/2}\,\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{1/2}\,\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{1/2}\,\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}^{2}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{1/2}\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\nabla\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-3/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}^{2}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}+\epsilon^{-1/2}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\nabla\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\epsilon^{-3/4}\,\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}+\epsilon^{1/4}\,\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}+\epsilon^{1/4}\,\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\hat{\xi}\bigr\|_{L^{4}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\|\hat{\xi}\|_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\|\hat{\xi}\|_{\epsilon}\Bigl(1+\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|+\epsilon^{-1}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}+\epsilon^{-1}\bigl\|\nabla_{t}\bigl(\nabla\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\bigl\|\nabla\hat{J}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{s}\hat{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\bigl\|\nabla_{s}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl\|\bigr)=:C^{1}_{\mathcal{F}},

where we again crucially use the fact that on the shrinking strip the almost complex structure
\hat{J}
and base point
\hat{u}
are constant in
t\in[0,1]
so that we can cancel the terms with negative exponents of
\epsilon
due to

\nabla_{t}\bigl(\hat{J}(\hat{u})\bigr)=0
and
\nabla_{t}\bigl(\nabla\hat{J}\,\partial_{s}\hat{u}\bigr)=0
.

This finishes the sketch of proof of Theorem
3.4
up to the following proof of its key lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

The linearization of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
or
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
at
0
with respect to any Sobolev norm is

{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)\,:\;(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\mapsto\bigl(\;{\rm D}_{u_{02}}\xi_{02}\;\;,\;\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}\;\bigr),

where

{\rm D}_{u_{02}}\xi_{02}:=\nabla_{t}\xi_{02}-J_{02}(u_{02})\nabla_{s}\xi_{02}-\nabla_{\xi_{02}}J_{02}\partial_{s}u_{02}
is a linearized Cauchy-Riemann operator at
u_{02}
,
and the boundary conditions encoded in the domain
\Gamma
are

(16)

\xi_{02}|_{t=1}\in{\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=1}}(L_{0}\times L_{2}),\hskip 10.00002pt\xi_{02}|_{t=0}=\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0},\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}_{11}|_{t=0}\in{\rm T}_{\hat{u}_{11}}\Delta_{1},\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}|_{t=1}\in{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}L_{0211}.

To understand the kernel, note that for
\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}=0
the boundary conditions reduce to

(17)

\xi_{02}|_{t=1}\in{\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=1}}(L_{0}\times L_{2}),\hskip 20.00003pt\xi_{02}|_{t=0}\in{\rm Pr}_{02}\bigl({\rm T}_{\hat{u}}(M_{02}^{-}\times\Delta_{1})\cap{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}L_{0211}\bigr)={\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=0}}L_{02}

with the notation
{\rm T}_{u_{02}(s,0)}L_{02}:={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}({\rm T}_{\hat{u}(s)}\widetilde{L}_{02})
from
Remark
3.2
.
Then the remaining boundary condition
\xi_{02}|_{t=0}=\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}
determines
\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}\in{\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=0}}L_{02}
, which – via the isomorphisms in Remark
3.1
and (
15
) uniquely determines
\hat{\xi}=\hat{\xi}(s)
as
\hat{\xi}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}(\xi_{02}|_{t=0})
.

Thus the kernel of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)
is identified with solutions of
{\rm D}_{u_{02}}\xi_{02}=0
with boundary conditions (
17
) – that is, by the kernel of the linearized operator for two strips, which is a Fredholm operator

{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\,:\;\bigl\{\xi_{02}\in H^{s+1}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})\,\big|\,\eqref{eq:bc 0}\bigr\}\;\to\;H^{s}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02}).

Here the choice of Sobolev regularity
s\geq 0
is immaterial – by elliptic regularity the kernel is always the same finite dimensional space of smooth sections.
Thus we have identified the kernel as

\ker\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}\;=\;\ker{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)\;=\;\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k}_{02},\hat{\mathfrak{k}})\,\big|\,\mathfrak{k}_{02}\in\ker{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag},\hat{\mathfrak{k}}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\circ\mathfrak{k}_{02}|_{t=0}\bigr\}\;\subset\;\Gamma.

Similarly, the cokernel of
{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}
is independent of
s
. More precisely, the
L^{2}
-orthogonal complement
\mathfrak{C}_{02}:=(\operatorname{im}{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag})^{\perp}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})
consists of smooth sections by elliptic regularity,
so that we have a direct sum

H^{s}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})\;=\;\operatorname{im}{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\oplus\mathfrak{C}_{02}
for all
s\geq 0
.
Yet another equivalent Fredholm operator for this boundary value problem is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\,:\;\{\xi_{02}\in H^{3/2}\,|\,\xi_{02}|_{t=1}\in{\rm T}(L_{0}\times L_{2})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\;\to\;H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R},{\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=0}}L_{02}^{\perp}),

\displaystyle\xi_{02}\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\;\mapsto\;\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(\;{\rm D}_{u_{02}}\xi_{02}\;,\;\pi_{02}^{\perp}\circ\xi_{02}|_{t=0}\;\bigr).

This encodes the
t=0
boundary condition in the operator by restriction
H^{3/2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1])\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}),\xi_{02}\mapsto\xi_{02}|_{t=0}
and projection to the orthogonal complement of the immersed Lagrangian tangent spaces
\pi_{02}^{\perp}:{\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=0}}M_{02}\to({\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=0}}L_{02})^{\perp}:=\bigl({\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}\widetilde{L}_{02}\bigr)^{\perp}
as in Remark
3.2
.
This operator has the same kernel as
{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}
and isomorphic cokernel, so that we obtain the direct sum

(18)

H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R},{\rm T}_{u_{02}|_{t=0}}L_{02}^{\perp})\;=\;\operatorname{im}\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\;\oplus\;\{(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)\,|\,\mathfrak{c}_{02}\in\mathfrak{C}_{02}\}.

Now we can establish a representation of the cokernel of
\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}
by proving the direct sum

\displaystyle H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})\times\bigl(L^{1}([0,1],H^{1}(\mathbb{R},{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}\hat{M}))\cap W^{1,1}([0,1],L^{2}(\mathbb{R},{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}\hat{M}))\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\;=\;\operatorname{im}\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}\;\oplus\;\{(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)\,|\,\mathfrak{c}_{02}\in\mathfrak{C}_{02}\}.

To do so we rewrite the sum claim for any given
(\eta_{02},\hat{\eta})
in the left hand side

\displaystyle(\eta_{02},\hat{\eta})=\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})+(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0),\hskip 20.00003pt(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})\in\overline{\Gamma}_{0}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\eta_{02}={\rm D}_{u_{02}}\xi_{02}+\mathfrak{c}_{02},\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\eta}=\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi},\hskip 20.00003pt\xi_{02}\in H^{3/2},\hat{\xi}\in W^{1,1}([0,1],H^{1})\cap W^{2,1}([0,1],L^{2}),\eqref{eq:bc Gamma}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{u_{02}}\xi_{02}=\eta_{02}-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\hskip 10.00002pt\nabla_{t}\hat{\xi}=\hat{\eta},\hskip 20.00003pt\eta_{02}\in{\rm dom}\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag},\hat{\eta}\in{\rm dom}\widetilde{\nabla}_{t},\hskip 10.00002pt\xi_{02}|_{t=0}=\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(\eta_{02}-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\pi_{02}^{\perp}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\widetilde{\nabla}_{t}\hat{\xi}=(\hat{\eta},\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}),\hskip 20.00003pt\eta_{02}\in{\rm dom}\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag},\hat{\eta}\in{\rm dom}\widetilde{\nabla}_{t}.

Here we split
\xi_{02}|_{t=0}=\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}\;\Leftrightarrow\;\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}=\pi_{02}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}\;\text{and}\;\pi_{02}^{\perp}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}=\pi_{02}^{\perp}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}
and recognized each of these conditions as boundary conditions encoded in the operators
\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{t}
.
Abstractly, the resulting equations are too coupled to have evident solutions. However, we can utilize the explicit inverse

(\hat{\eta},\lambda)\mapsto{\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\lambda+\int_{0}^{\bullet}\hat{\eta}-(\pi_{02}^{\perp}\times\pi_{11})\int\hat{\eta}

of
\widetilde{\nabla}_{t}
from Remark
3.1
to solve the equations:
Applied to
(\eta_{02},\hat{\eta})=(0,0)
the equivalence identifies the intersection of the subspaces

\displaystyle\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})=(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)\hskip 20.00003pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\pi_{02}^{\perp}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\widetilde{\nabla}_{t}\hat{\xi}=(0,\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\pi_{02}^{\perp}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(-\mathfrak{c}_{02},0),\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\xi}_{02}=0,\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\pi_{02}\,0=0.

And given

(\eta_{02},\hat{\eta})\in H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],{\rm T}_{u_{02}}M_{02})\times\bigl(L^{1}([0,1],H^{1}(\mathbb{R},{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}\hat{M}))\cap W^{1,1}([0,1],L^{2}(\mathbb{R},{\rm T}_{\hat{u}}\hat{M}))\bigr)
we can solve

\displaystyle(\eta_{02},\hat{\eta})=\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})+(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(\eta_{02}-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\pi_{02}^{\perp}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\widetilde{\nabla}_{t}\hat{\xi}=(\hat{\eta},\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(\eta_{02}-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\pi_{02}^{\perp}\hat{\xi}_{02}|_{t=0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}+\textstyle\int_{0}^{\bullet}\hat{\eta}-(\pi_{02}^{\perp}\times\pi_{11})\int\hat{\eta}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}\xi_{02}=(\eta_{02}-\mathfrak{c}_{02},\pi_{02}^{\perp}\textstyle\int\hat{\eta}_{11}),\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\xi}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}L_{02}}^{-1}\pi_{02}\xi_{02}|_{t=0}+\textstyle\int_{0}^{\bullet}\hat{\eta}

by first finding a (not necessarily unique) pair
(\xi_{02},\mathfrak{c}_{02})
that solves the stabilized equation for
\widetilde{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}
and then computing
\hat{\xi}
from the above formula.
This proves the claimed direct sum and thus identifies the cokernel of
\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}
. Since both kernel and cokernel are finite dimensional, this establishes
\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}
as a Fredholm operator – with index equal to the index of
{\rm D}_{u_{02}}^{\rm Lag}
.

Finally, the stabilized Fredholm estimate follows from the fact that the linear operator

(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi},\mathfrak{c}_{02})\mapsto\bigl(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}_{02}}(\xi_{02}),\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}(\xi_{02},\hat{\xi})-(\mathfrak{c}_{02},0)\bigr)

is bounded, surjective, and has Fredholm index
0
– hence has a bounded inverse.
∎

3.2.
Instanton and Symplectic Floer Theory

There are several conjectures of Atiyah-Floer type, which all relate the instanton Floer homology of a
3
-manifold – defined from moduli spaces of anti-self-dual instantons – to a symplectic Floer homology – defined from moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic maps to a representation space arising from dimensional reduction of the anti-self-duality equation.

In the nondegenerate Atiyah-Floer conjecture
HF^{\rm inst}(Y_{h})\simeq HF(\phi_{f})
proved in
[
DS94
]
the
3
-manifold is the mapping torus
Y_{h}
of a diffeomorphism
h:\Sigma\to\Sigma
of a closed Riemann surface. It is induced by a bundle automorphism
f:Q\to Q
of a nontrivial
G=SO(3)
-bundle
Q\to\Sigma
, which induces a nontrivial bundle
Q_{f}\to Y_{h}
and the differential for the instanton Floer homology counts anti-self-dual connections on
\mathbb{R}\times Q_{f}
.
The bundle automorphism also induces a symplectomorphism
\phi_{f}:\mathcal{R}(Q)\to\mathcal{R}(Q)
of the moduli space
\mathcal{R}(Q)
of flat connections on
Q
(which can be identified with a
G
-representation space), and the differential for the symplectic Floer homology counts pseudoholomorphic maps
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\mathcal{R}(Q)
with the boundary values matching via
\phi_{f}
.

In the (original) Atiyah-Floer conjecture
HF^{\rm inst}(Y)\simeq HF^{\rm symp}(L_{H_{0}},L_{H_{1}})
the
3
-manifold is a homology
3
-sphere
Y
. Then the instanton Floer differential counts anti-self-dual connections on the trivial
G=SU(2)
bundle over
\mathbb{R}\times Y
.
A choice of Heegard splitting
Y=H_{0}\cup_{\Sigma}H_{1}
gives rise to a singular representation space
\mathcal{R}(\Sigma)
with two Lagrangians
L_{H_{0}},L_{H_{1}}\subset\mathcal{R}(\Sigma)
whose intersection points correspond to flat connections on
Y
. Then the – only conjecturally defined – Lagrangian Floer homology is thought to be counting pseudoholomorphic maps
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\mathcal{R}(\Sigma)
with boundary values in
L_{H_{0}}
and
L_{H_{1}}
.

The adiabatic limit approach to proving these relationships was developed by Salamon
[
Sal95
]
.
After several localizations – in the moduli space, in a local slice (centered at a reference connection
A_{0}+\Phi_{0}{\rm d}s+\Psi_{0}{\rm d}t
), and locally on the domain – it studies the family of PDEs for
0<\epsilon\leq 1

(19)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}A-{\rm d}_{A}\Phi+*\bigl(\partial_{t}A-*{\rm d}_{A}\Psi\bigr)&=0,\\
\partial_{s}\Psi-\partial_{t}\Phi+[\Phi,\Psi]+\varepsilon^{-2}*F_{A}&=0,\\
\nabla^{0}_{s}(\Phi-\Phi_{0})+\nabla^{0}_{t}(\Psi-\Psi_{0})+\varepsilon^{-2}*{\rm d}_{A_{0}}*(A-A_{0})&=0\end{aligned}\right.

for a triple of maps
{A:[-1,1]\times[0,1]\to\Omega^{1}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q})}

and
{\Phi,\Psi:[-1,1]\times[0,1]\to\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q})}
to spaces of
1
- resp.
0
-forms with values in the associated bundle
\mathfrak{g}_{Q}=Q\times_{\rm Ad}\mathfrak{g}
.
Their energy

\mathcal{E}(A,\Phi,\Psi)=\int_{[-1,1]\times[0,1]\times\Sigma}|\partial_{s}A-{\rm d}_{A}\Phi|^{2}+\varepsilon^{-2}|F_{A}|^{2}

is bounded, independently of
\epsilon
, by a global monotonicity formula.
Thus the expectation is to obtain in the
\epsilon\to 0
limit
a triple of maps
{A:[-1,1]\times[0,1]\to\Omega^{1}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q})}

and
{\Phi,\Psi:[-1,1]\times[0,1]\to\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q})}
satisfying

(20)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}A-{\rm d}_{A}\Phi+*\bigl(\partial_{t}A-{\rm d}_{A}\Psi\bigr)&=0,\\
*F_{A}&=0,\\
{\rm d}_{A_{0}}*(A-A_{0})&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Such an
\epsilon=0
solution can then be interpreted as a map
[A]:[-1,1]\times[0,1]\to\mathcal{R}(Q)
to the moduli space of flat connections on
Q
that solves the Cauchy-Riemann equation with respect to the almost complex structure
J
induced by the Hodge
*
operator

\partial_{s}[A]+J([A])\partial_{t}[A]=0.

Our proposal for casting this classical adiabatic limit as an adiabatic Fredholm family is to multiply (
19
) by
\epsilon^{2}
and change variables to
\epsilon^{\rm new}=(\epsilon^{\rm old})^{2}
to obtain a family of PDEs for
0\leq\epsilon\leq 1
which naturally combines (
19
) and (
20
),

\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\alpha,\varphi,\psi)\,:=\;\left(\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}A-{\rm d}_{A}\Phi+*\bigl(\partial_{t}A-*{\rm d}_{A}\Psi\bigr)\\
\varepsilon\bigl(\partial_{s}\Psi-\partial_{t}\Phi+[\Phi,\Psi]\bigr)+*F_{A}\\
\varepsilon\bigl(\nabla^{0}_{s}(\Phi-\Phi_{0})+\nabla^{0}_{t}(\Psi-\Psi_{0})\bigr)+*{\rm d}_{A_{0}}*(A-A_{0})\end{aligned}\right)\;=\;0,

where
A=A_{0}+\alpha,\Phi=\Phi_{0}+\phi,\Psi=\Psi_{0}+\psi
.
This formulates the adiabatic limit near a solution
A_{0}+\Phi_{0}{\rm d}s+\Psi_{0}{\rm d}t
and locally on
Z:=[-1,1]\times[0,1]
– as a family of maps
(\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon\in[0,1]}
with common domain and target space

\Gamma\,:=\;\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(Z,\Omega^{1}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q}))\times\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(Z,\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q}))\times\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(Z,\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}_{Q}))\;=:\,\Omega.

The norms for
\epsilon>0
from
[
DS94
]
become

\displaystyle\|(\alpha,\phi,\psi)\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\,:=\;\epsilon^{-1/2}\|\alpha\|_{L^{p}(Z,W^{1,p}(\Sigma))}+\|\nabla_{Z}\alpha\|_{L^{p}}+\|(\varphi,\psi)\|_{L^{p}(Z,W^{1,p}(\Sigma))}+\epsilon^{1/2}\|(\varphi,\psi)\|_{L^{p}},

\displaystyle\|(\alpha,\phi,\psi)\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\,:=\;\|\alpha\|_{L^{p}}+\epsilon^{-1/2}\|(\varphi,\psi)\|_{L^{p}}.

Now the key step is to find norms
\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma}_{0}\leq\|(\alpha,\phi,\psi)\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}
and

\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{0}\leq\|(\alpha,\phi,\psi)\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
which give the
\epsilon=0
linearized operator the Fredholm property

\overline{{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{0}(0)}:(\alpha,\varphi,\psi)\;\mapsto\;\left(\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}\alpha-{\rm d}_{A_{0}}\Phi+*\bigl(\partial_{t}\alpha-*{\rm d}_{A_{0}}\psi\bigr)\\
*{\rm d}_{A_{0}}\alpha\qquad\;\;\\
*{\rm d}_{A_{0}}*\alpha\qquad\end{aligned}\right).

We have a conjecture that we would be happy to share with folks who are interested in making righteous use of this adiabatic limt.

Conjecture 3.5
.

The above data

\bigl((\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\to\Omega)_{\epsilon\in[0,1]},\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)

can be supplemented
to form a regularizing
\mathcal{C}^{1}
-regular adiabatic Fredholm family as in Definition
2.1
,
2.13
.

4.
Proofs

This section proves Theorem
1.8
.
To make the strategy of this proof more accessible, we will work through each step first in the case of a classical Fredholm section – reproving the following classical fact, which underlies most constructions of invariants from moduli spaces of PDEs.

Theorem 4.1
.

Suppose an open subset
\mathcal{U}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}
of a topological space (such as a compactified moduli space) is described as the zero set of a
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular Fredholm map
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to\overline{\Omega}
for
\ell\geq 1
. More precisely, we assume the following:

•

(\overline{\Gamma},\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma})
and
(\overline{\Omega},\|\cdot\|^{\Omega})
are Banach spaces.

•

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\subset\overline{\Gamma}
is a an open neighbourhood of
0=0_{\overline{\Gamma}}\in\overline{\Gamma}
.

•

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to\overline{\Omega}
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-map with
\mathcal{F}(0)=0
, whose linearization
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0):\overline{\Gamma}\to\overline{\Omega}
is Fredholm, that is,
\mathfrak{K}:=\ker{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
and
\mathfrak{C}:=\operatorname{coker}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)=\overline{\Omega}/\operatorname{im}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
are finite dimensional.

•

\psi:\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{U}
is a homeomorphism.

Then any choice of projection
\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}:\overline{\Gamma}\to\mathfrak{K}
and inclusion
\mathfrak{C}\subset\overline{\Omega}
such that
\overline{\Omega}=\mathfrak{C}\oplus\operatorname{im}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
provides a Fredholm stabilization of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
in the sense that for some constant
C_{0}
we have

\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}+\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}\leq C_{0}\bigl(\|\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)\|^{\Gamma}+\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}\bigr)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all}\;(\gamma,\mathfrak{c})\in\overline{\Gamma}\times\mathfrak{C}.

And this induces a finite dimensional reduction that describes
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
locally as the zero set of a map between finite dimensional spaces,

f:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathfrak{C},\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003pt\psi_{f}:f^{-1}(0)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}

defined on an open subset
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathfrak{K}
. It describes
\mathcal{U}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}
locally by composition
\psi_{f}=\psi\circ\phi
with a homeomorphism for some
\delta_{\sigma}>0

\phi\,:\;f^{-1}(0)\;\overset{\sim}{\to}\;\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)\cap\bigl\{\gamma\in\overline{\Gamma}\,\big|\,\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}<\delta_{\sigma}\bigr\}.

Moreover, this finite dimensional reduction is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
in the sense that the differentials of order
0\leq k\leq\ell
(see Remark
2.3
) form continuous maps

\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{L}^{k}(\mathfrak{K}^{k},\mathfrak{C})
,
\mathfrak{k}_{0}\mapsto{\rm D}^{k}f(\mathfrak{k}_{0})
.

The traditional approach (see e.g.
[
Weh12
, Rmk.4.2]
) to this proof is based on the
Fredholm splitting
given by the direct sums

(21)

\overline{\Gamma}=\mathfrak{K}\oplus\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003pt\overline{\Omega}=\mathfrak{C}\oplus\operatorname{im}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)

and the fact that the linearization restricts to an isomorphism
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)|_{\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}}:\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\operatorname{im}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
. One then uses its inverse
Q:=({\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)|_{\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}})^{-1}
to rewrite for
\gamma=\mathfrak{k}+w\in\mathfrak{K}\oplus\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}=\overline{\Gamma}

\mathcal{F}(\gamma)=0\hskip 20.00003pt\Longleftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt\pi_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{F}(\mathfrak{k}+w))=0\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptQ\,({\rm Id}_{\overline{\Omega}}-\pi_{\mathfrak{C}})\,\mathcal{F}(\mathfrak{k}+w)=0.

Here the second equation for sufficiently small
\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
is a fixed point equation for a retraction on
\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}
by
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity of
\mathcal{F}
. The unique fixed points then define a solution map
\sigma:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}
such that

Q\,({\rm Id}_{\overline{\Omega}}-\pi_{\mathfrak{C}})\,\mathcal{F}(\mathfrak{k}+w)=0\;\Leftrightarrow\;w=\sigma(\mathfrak{k})
and hence we obtain the desired finite dimensional reduction
f:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
\phi:f^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)
from

\mathcal{F}(\gamma)=0\hskip 20.00003pt\Longleftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt\gamma=\phi(\mathfrak{k}):=\mathfrak{k}+\sigma(\mathfrak{k})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{with}\hskip 10.00002ptf(\mathfrak{k}):=\pi_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{F}(\mathfrak{k}+\sigma(\mathfrak{k}))=0.

This formulation, however, is not suitable for the adiabatic analysis, as it heavily depends on direct sums – which are hard to formulate on dense subsets with
\epsilon
-dependent norms. To remedy this, we will in §
4.1
replace in the Fredholm splitting by a
Fredholm stabilization isomorphism

(22)

\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}\;\rightarrow\;\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega},\hskip 20.00003pt(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\;\mapsto\;(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}).

The remaining proof proceeds analogous to the above outline:

\cdot

§
4.2
uses the inverse of this isomorphism to rewrite
\mathcal{F}(\gamma)=0
as a finite dimensional equation and a fixed point problem for a family of contractions.

\cdot

§
4.3
establishes the solution maps for this fixed point problem, in particular proves their
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity.

\cdot

§
4.4
deduces the finite dimensional reduction given the solution map and the remaining finite dimensional equation.

A useful zero-th step in this approach is to identify the zero set of the nonlinear map
\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)\simeq\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)
with the zero set of a stabilized map of the same Fredholm index.

Lemma 4.2
.

Consider any map
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}\to\overline{\Omega}
from a set
\mathcal{V}
to a vector space
\overline{\Omega}
.
Given any auxiliary map
\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}:\mathcal{V}\to\mathfrak{K}
to another vector space
\mathfrak{K}
and auxiliary subspace
\mathfrak{C}\subset\overline{\Omega}
, the zero set
\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)
is naturally identified with the zero set of the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\;\rightarrow\;\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega},

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)

\displaystyle\;\mapsto\;(\mathfrak{c},\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\mathcal{F}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c}).

Proof.

The identification is given by the map
(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)\mapsto\gamma
with inverse
\gamma\mapsto(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)
.
∎

This is the idea that we use to “externalize” the finite dimensional factors
\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{C}
when dealing with adiabatic Fredholm families.

Lemma 4.3
.

Given an adiabatic Fredholm family
\bigl((\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\to\Omega)_{\epsilon\in\Delta},\ldots\bigr)
as in Definition
2.1
, its union of zero sets
\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\Delta\times\Gamma
is naturally identified with the zero set of the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}:\Delta\times\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\;\rightarrow\;\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega,

\displaystyle(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)

\displaystyle\;\mapsto\;(\mathfrak{c},\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c}).

The same holds for the extended adiabatic Fredholm family, that is
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
is naturally identified with
\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)\subset\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
. More precisely, the union of zero sets
\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\simeq\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(0_{\Delta})
is identified with the preimage of the "zero section"
0_{\Delta}:=\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\{(0,0,0)\}\subset\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
under the "section"

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}:\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},

\displaystyle(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathfrak{c},\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c}).

In both cases, the identification for fixed
\epsilon\in\Delta
is given by the map
(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)\mapsto\gamma
with inverse
\gamma\mapsto(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)
; in the first case this restricts to
\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\subset\Gamma
with
\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}|_{\Gamma\subset\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}=\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}
.

If, moreover, the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing in the sense of Definition
2.8
, then the zero sets of the extended adiabatic Fredholm family agree with the original zero sets
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)=\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
for each
\epsilon\in\Delta
and
\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(0)=\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)\subset\Delta\times\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

By construction, the zero set
\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)\subset\Delta\times\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
is the subset of
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}=\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),\mathfrak{c}=0,\gamma)
with
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0
, thus a bijection to
\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
is given by
(\epsilon,\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)\mapsto(\epsilon,\gamma)
with inverse
(\epsilon,\gamma)\mapsto(\epsilon,\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)
.

For fixed
\epsilon\in\Delta
, these maps are uniformly continuous w.r.t. the
\epsilon
-dependent norm on
\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
, hence extend to the claimed natural bijection between
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
and
\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)
as in Lemma
2.4
.
These are the fibres of the preimage of the "zero section"
\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(0_{\Delta})=\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)
.

Now suppose that the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing, in particular that we have the implication
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)\in\Omega\;\Rightarrow\;\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
. Then every
\gamma\in\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
must lie in
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
since
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0
lies in
\Omega
, so that the regularizing property implies
\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
with
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0
.
Analogously,
(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)\subset\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
implies
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{C}\subset\Omega
, and hence
\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
with
\mathcal{G}(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=0
.
This proves the inclusions

\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
and
\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)\subset\mathcal{G}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)\subset\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
, the converse inclusions hold by the construction of
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}
resp.
\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)
as extensions of
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
resp.
\mathcal{G}(\epsilon,\cdot)
.
∎

4.1.
Uniform Inverses

This section constructs the analogue of the right inverse used in the Newton-Picard Iteration of classical adiabatic limit methods. Conceptually, our inverse operators will arise from the fact that any Fredholm splitting (
21
) is equivalent to a Fredholm stabilization (
22
). Moreover, we will quantify the continuity of the resulting inverse operators when varying the base point, which will play a crucial role in establishing regularity of the finite dimensional charts resulting from an adiabatic Fredholm family.

Starting in the classical Fredholm setting of Theorem
4.1
, we start by noting that any
\mathcal{C}^{1}
Fredholm map satisfies an estimate that – for our present purposes – can play the role of the [Quadratic-ish Estimate] and [Uniform Continuity of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
] in Definitions
2.1
and
2.13
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Any Fredholm map
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to\Omega
as in Theorem
4.1
satisfies a
[Quadratic-ish Estimate]

(23)

\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})\gamma-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma\bigr\|^{\Omega}\leq\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})\|\gamma\|^{\overline{\Gamma}}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}},\gamma\in\Gamma,

where
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to[0,\infty)
is a continuous map that vanishes on the diagonal
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{0})=0
. If, moreover,
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
is convex and
\|{\rm D}^{2}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}^{2},\overline{\Omega})}\leq C^{2}_{\mathcal{F}}
is bounded for
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
,
then
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})\leq C^{2}_{\mathcal{F}}\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}-\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}
makes (
23
) a truly quadratic estimate.
Further, there is a constant
\delta_{Q}>0
so that for any
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
with
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}
we have a
Fredholm stabilization isomorphism

(24)

\displaystyle P(\gamma_{0})\,:\;\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega},

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}).

Its inverse operators
Q(\gamma_{0}):=P(\gamma_{0})^{-1}:\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}
are uniformly bounded – where we equip
\mathfrak{C}\subset\overline{\Omega}
and
\mathfrak{K}\subset\overline{\Gamma}
with the induced norms
\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}:=\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{\Gamma}
resp.
\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}:=\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}
–

(25)

\displaystyle\|Q(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma},\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{Q}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}},\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}.

Moreover, the inverse operators
Q(\gamma_{0})
vary continuously with
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
. More precisely, for all

\gamma_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
with
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma},\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}
we have with
c:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to[0,\infty)
from (
23
)

(26)

\|Q(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})-Q(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega},\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma})}\leq(C_{Q})^{2}\;\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}).

Proof.

To begin, the
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity of
\mathcal{F}
implies a [Quadratic-ish Estimate] for
\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}},\gamma\in\Gamma
,

\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})\gamma-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma\bigr\|^{\Omega}\;\leq\;\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Omega})}\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}\;=\;\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}

with
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to[0,\infty)

given by
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}):=\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Omega})}
.
This map evidently vanishes on the diagonal
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{0})=0
, and

\mathcal{C}^{0}
regularity of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to\mathcal{L}(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Omega})
exactly is the statement that the map
c
is continuous.
If, moreover,
\|{\rm D}^{2}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}^{2},\overline{\Omega})}\leq C^{2}_{\mathcal{F}}
is uniformly bounded for
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
, then – utilizing the convexity of
\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
– we have as claimed

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Omega})}\;=\;\bigl\|\textstyle\int_{0}^{1}{\rm D}[{\rm D}\mathcal{F}](\gamma_{0}+\lambda(\gamma_{0}^{\prime}-\gamma_{0}))\;(\gamma_{0}^{\prime}-\gamma)\;{\rm d}\lambda\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Omega})}

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle\int_{0}^{1}\bigl\|{\rm D}^{2}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}+\lambda(\gamma_{0}^{\prime}-\gamma_{0}))\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}^{2},\overline{\Omega})}{\rm d}\lambda\;\bigl\|\gamma_{0}^{\prime}-\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\Gamma}\;\leq\;C^{2}_{\mathcal{F}}\,\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}-\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}.

Next, for
\gamma_{0}=0
we can show injectivity of
P(0)
as follows:
Given
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\ker P(0)
, the direct sum
\overline{\Omega}=\mathfrak{C}\oplus\operatorname{im}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
and
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}=0
imply
\mathfrak{c}=0
and
\gamma\in\ker{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
.
Now the direct sum
\overline{\Gamma}=\mathfrak{K}\oplus\ker\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}
together with
\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)=0
implies
\gamma=0
.

Moreover,
P(0)
is a stabilization of the Fredholm map
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)
in the sense that it result by Cartesian product with finite dimensional factors in the domain and codomain, and addition of a linear operator
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),-\mathfrak{c})\in\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}
composed with the compact embedding
\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\subset\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}
.
Here the embedding
\mathfrak{C}\hookrightarrow\overline{\Omega}
is compact since
\mathfrak{C}
is finite dimensional.
Thus
P(0)
is a Fredholm operator of index

{\operatorname{ind}}\,P(0)\;=\;{\operatorname{ind}}\,\bigl((\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(0,{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma)\bigr)\;=\;{\operatorname{ind}}\,{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)+\dim\mathfrak{C}-\dim\mathfrak{K}\;=\;0.

Since
P(0)
is injective, the Fredholm index
0
guarantees that
P(0)
is also surjective, and hence a bijection. Since
P(0)
is a bounded operator, its inverse
Q(0):=P(0)^{-1}:\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}
is continuous as well, that is we have a bound with a preliminary constant
C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}
,

(27)

\displaystyle\|Q(0)(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\|^{\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}\|(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\in\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}.

This invertibility extends to
\gamma_{0}\neq 0
whenever
P(\gamma_{0})
is sufficiently close to
P(0)
in the space of bounded linear operators. This proximity is controlled by (
23
): For all
\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|P(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})-P(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma},\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}\leq 1}\bigl\|\bigl(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\bigr)-\bigl(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\bigr)\bigr\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}}

(28)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}\leq 1}\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\gamma-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma\bigr\|^{\Omega}\;\leq\;\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}).

Set
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}=0
here, then continuity of
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to[0,\infty)
ensures an
\delta_{Q}>0
so that
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}
implies

\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(0,\gamma_{0})=\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(0,\gamma_{0})-\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(0,0)\leq\frac{1}{2C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}}
and hence

C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}\bigl\|P(0)-P(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma},\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega})}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Then a classical construction yields uniformly bounded inverses of
P_{1}:=P(\gamma_{0})
from the inverses
Q_{0}:=Q(0)
of
P_{0}:=P(0)
whenever
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
satisfies
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}
as follows: First the composed operator

T:=Q_{0}(P_{0}-P_{1}):\overline{W}\to\overline{W}
on
\overline{W}:=\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}
is small in the sense that

(29)

\displaystyle\|T\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|Q_{0}\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega},\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma})}\bigl\|P_{0}-P_{1}\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma},\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}\,\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(0,\gamma_{0})\;\leq\;\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}},\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}.

With that we can express
P_{1}=P_{0}({\rm Id}-T)

since
Q_{0}
is the left and right inverse of
P_{0}
and thus

P_{0}({\rm Id}-T)\;=\;P_{0}-P_{0}Q_{0}(P_{0}-P_{1})\;=\;P_{0}-(P_{0}-P_{1})\;=\;P_{1}.

In this decomposition,
({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}T^{n}
exists with operator norm bounded by

(30)

\textstyle\bigl\|({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}\;\leq\;\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\|T\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})})^{n}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{1-\|T\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}}\;\leq\;2.

Now
Q_{1}:=({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}Q_{0}
is the left and right inverse of
P_{1}
since

\displaystyle Q_{1}P_{1}

\displaystyle=\;({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}Q_{0}P_{0}({\rm Id}-T)\;=\;({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\bigl({\rm Id}-T\bigr)\;=\;{\rm Id}

and

\displaystyle P_{1}Q_{1}

\displaystyle=\;P_{0}({\rm Id}-T)({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}Q_{0}\;=\;P_{0}Q_{0}\;=\;{\rm Id}.

Going back to unabbreviated notation, this shows that
P(\gamma_{0})=P_{1}
is invertible for all
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
,
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}
with inverses
Q(\gamma_{0}):=P(\gamma_{0})^{-1}=Q_{1}
that satisfy the uniform bound

\displaystyle\|Q(\gamma_{0})(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\|^{\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|({\rm Id}_{\overline{W}}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}\bigl\|Q(0)(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega,\overline{W})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}\,\bigl\|(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\bigr\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}\;=\;C_{Q}\,\bigl\|(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\bigr\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}.

This confirms (
34
) with the constant
C_{Q}=2\,C^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm prelim}_{Q}
.

Next, we will show that these inverses vary continuously with
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
. For that purpose we abbreviate
\overline{V}:=\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}
and consider
\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
with
\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\|^{\Gamma},\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}
to compare the classically constructed inverses of
P_{1}:=P(\gamma_{0})
and
P^{\prime}_{1}:=P(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})
with
\|P^{\prime}_{1}-P_{1}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{V})}\leq\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})
by (
28
).
These inverses are

Q_{1}:=Q(\gamma_{0})=({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}Q_{0}
with
T=Q_{0}(P_{0}-P_{1})
and

Q^{\prime}_{1}:=Q(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})=({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}Q_{0}
with
T^{\prime}=Q_{0}(P_{0}-P^{\prime}_{1})
, where
P_{0}=P(0)
and
Q_{0}=Q(0)
as above.
So to estimate
\|Q^{\prime}_{1}-Q_{1}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{V},\overline{W})}
we first keep track of

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}-T\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle=\|Q_{0}(P_{0}-P^{\prime}_{1})-Q_{0}(P_{0}-P_{1})\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle=\|Q_{0}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{V},\overline{W})}\|P_{1}-P^{\prime}_{1}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{V})}\;\leq\;\tfrac{1}{2}C_{Q}\,\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}),

where we used (
28
) and
\|Q_{0}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{V},\overline{W})}\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{Q}
from (
27
).
Next we use the classical fact that inverting linear operators is a continuous map. In particular the following
map is uniformly continuous

\bigl\{T\in\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})\,\big|\,\|T\|<1\bigr\}\to\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W}),\hskip 20.00003ptT\mapsto({\rm Id}_{\overline{W}}-T)^{-1}=\textstyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}T^{n}.

Indeed, we can use the fact that
T
commutes with
({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}T^{n}
to estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}-({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|

\displaystyle=\bigl\|({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}({\rm Id}-T)-({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|

\displaystyle=\bigl\|-({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}T+T^{\prime}({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|

(31)

\displaystyle=\bigl\|({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}(T^{\prime}-T)({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}T\bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|({\rm Id}-T^{\prime})^{-1}\bigr\|\|T^{\prime}-T\|\bigl\|({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\|T^{\prime}\|}\|T^{\prime}-T\|\tfrac{1}{1-\|T\|}.

Here we bounded the norms of inverses by
\|({\rm Id}-T)^{-1}\|=\|\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}T^{n}\|\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\|T\|^{n}=\tfrac{1}{1-\|T\|}
, and we can further use (
29
) to bound
\|T^{\prime}\|,\|T\|\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Putting this all together we obtain for any
\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
with
\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\|^{\Gamma},\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq\delta_{Q}

\displaystyle\|Q(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})-Q(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{V},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|({\rm Id}_{\overline{W}}-T^{\prime})^{-1}Q_{0}-({\rm Id}_{\overline{W}}-T)^{-1}Q_{0}\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{V},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|({\rm Id}_{\overline{W}}-T^{\prime})^{-1}-({\rm Id}_{\overline{W}}-T)^{-1}\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}\|Q_{0}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{V},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\frac{1}{2}C_{Q}}{(1-\|T^{\prime}\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})})(1-\|T\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})})}\;\|T^{\prime}-T\|^{\mathcal{L}(\overline{W},\overline{W})}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\frac{1}{2}C_{Q}}{(1-\frac{1}{2})(1-\frac{1}{2})}\;\tfrac{1}{2}C_{Q}\;\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})\;=\;(C_{Q})^{2}\;\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}).

This confirms (
35
), and then continuity of
\gamma_{0}\mapsto Q(\gamma_{0})
follows from
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})\to 0
for
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\to\gamma_{0}
.
∎

When implementing this analysis for an adiabatic Fredholm family, it is instructive to recognize the Fredholm stabilization isomorphism (
24
) for
\mathcal{F}=\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}
as a differential of the map

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}:\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c})

which arises from the last two components of the map

\overline{\mathcal{G}}:\Delta\times\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega})_{\epsilon}

from Lemma
4.3
, that is we express

\mathcal{G}(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=(\mathfrak{c},\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c})=:(\mathfrak{c},\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}(\mathfrak{c},\gamma))
. In terms of solving the equation
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0\;\Leftrightarrow\;\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=0
, this means we split off the equation
\mathfrak{c}=0
, which will in §
4.4
give rise to the finite dimensional reduction
f:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathfrak{C}
– after §
4.3
solves the remaining equations

(32)

\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=0\hskip 20.00003pt\Longleftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{k}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{c}.

Alternatively, we can also understand
\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}
as the continuous extension of the maps

\displaystyle g_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}:\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega,\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c})

given for each
\epsilon\in\Delta
and
\mathfrak{k}\in\mathfrak{K}
by the last two components of the map

\mathcal{G}(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},\cdot,\cdot)
from Lemma
4.3
.
That is the context for generalizing Lemma
4.4
to adiabatic Fredholm families.
Note here moreover that the estimate (
23
) no longer even makes sense as we have no universal topology on
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
.
This is why we need to enhance the
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity of each Fredholm section
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}
by
the [Quadratic-ish Estimate] and [Uniform Continuity of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
] in Definitions
2.1
and
2.13
, which provide estimates that – with respect the
\epsilon
-dependent norms – are uniform in
\epsilon\in\Delta
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Given an adiabatic Fredholm family
\bigl((\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\to\Omega)_{\epsilon\in\Delta},\ldots\bigr)
as in Definition
2.1
, there exists a neighbourhood
\Delta_{Q}\subset\Delta
of
0
and a constant
\delta_{Q}>0
such that for any
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in\Delta_{Q}\times\mathfrak{K}
the linearizations of
\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}
at
(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
with
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{Q}
are isomorphisms

(33)

\displaystyle\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):={\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}):\;\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c})

whose inverse operators
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):=\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}:\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}
are uniformly bounded
16
16

16

Recall from Lemma
2.2
that we equip
\mathfrak{C}\subset\Omega
with the norm
\|\cdot\|^{\mathfrak{C}}:=\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{K}\subset\Gamma
with the norm
\|\cdot\|^{\mathfrak{K}}:=\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma}_{0}
.

(34)

\displaystyle\|\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\|^{\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C_{Q}\|(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}_{\epsilon}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon},\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{Q},(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\in\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega

by an
\epsilon
-independent constant
C_{Q}:=\max\{1,4(C_{1}+C_{0}+C_{0}C_{1}+C_{0}C_{\mathfrak{C}})\}
.

If the adiabatic Fredholm family satisfies
[Uniform Continuity of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
]
as in Definition
2.13
then the inverse operators
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})
vary uniformly continuously with
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
, that is for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
and

\gamma_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
with
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{Q}
we have

(35)

\|\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})-\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})}\leq(C_{Q})^{2}\;c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}-\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}),

where
c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
is the monotone continuous function from Definition
2.13
with
c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(0)=0
.

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing in the sense of Definition
2.8
, then the inverse operators at base points
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
restrict to bijections
Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):=\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})|_{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}:\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega\to\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
, which are the inverses of

\displaystyle P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):={\rm D}g_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}):\;\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega,

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}).

Proof.

First note that the linearizations

\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):={\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}):\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
are independent of the base point data
\mathfrak{k}_{0}\in\mathfrak{K}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}
, as they are given by
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c})
.

For
\gamma_{0}=0
and
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
to be determined we can prove injectivity of
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)
by starting from the [Uniform Fredholm-ish Estimate] in Lemma
2.2
to estimate for any
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+C_{1}\bigl(\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{0}\bigr)

using the triangle inequality for
\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+C_{1}\bigl(\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{0}\bigr)

using the [Uniform Cokernel Bound] in Lemma
2.2

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})\bigl(\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{0}+\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{c}}\bigr)

using the [
\epsilon=0
Fredholm Estimate] in Lemma
2.2

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}\bigl(\|\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{0}\bigr)

using the triangle inequality for
\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{0}
and the [Lower Bound on
\Omega
Norms] in Lemma
2.2

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}\|\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}\bigl(\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(0)\gamma-{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma\|^{\Omega}_{0}+\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)

using the [Continuity of Derivatives at
0
] in Lemma
2.2

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}\|\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}\bigl(c_{\Delta}(\epsilon)\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}+\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{1}+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0})\bigl(\|\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)+(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}c_{\Delta}(\epsilon)\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q}\|{\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},0)(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}_{\epsilon}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

with the preliminary constant
C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q}:=2(C_{1}+C_{0}+C_{0}C_{1}+C_{0}C_{\mathfrak{C}})
.
Here the last step chooses
\Delta_{Q}\subset\Delta
so that
c_{\Delta}(\epsilon)\leq\frac{1}{2(1+C_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{C}})C_{0}}
for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
.
Then we can rearrange this estimate to prove and quantify the injectivity of the linearization at base points
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}=0)
with
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
,

(36)

\displaystyle\|(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q}\|{\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},0)(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}_{\epsilon}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}.

Moreover, the linearizations
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)={\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},0):\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
at
\gamma_{0}=0
are stabilizations of the Fredholm maps
{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)
in the sense that they result by Cartesian product with finite dimensional factors in the domain and codomain, and addition of a linear operator
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),-\mathfrak{c})\in\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}
composed with the compact embedding
\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\subset\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
.
Here the embedding
\mathfrak{C}\hookrightarrow\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
is compact since
\mathfrak{C}
is finite dimensional and the embedding is continuous by the [Uniform Cokernel Bound] property.
Thus
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)
are Fredholm operators, and we can use the [Index] property
{\operatorname{ind}}\,{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)={\operatorname{ind}}\,{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(0)
to show that their Fredholm index is
0
,

\displaystyle{\operatorname{ind}}\,\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)

\displaystyle={\operatorname{ind}}\,\bigl((\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(0,{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma)\bigr)

\displaystyle={\operatorname{ind}}\,{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)+\dim\mathfrak{C}-\dim\mathfrak{K}\;=\;{\operatorname{ind}}\,{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)-{\operatorname{ind}}\,{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(0)\;=\;0.

Since we previously established that each
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)
is injective, the Fredholm index
0
guarantees that each
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)
is also surjective, and hence a bijection. Moreover, the above injectivity estimate (
36
) implies the uniform bound (
34
) for the inverse operators
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0):=\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)^{-1}:\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}
with the preliminary constant
C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q}
.

This invertibility extends to
\gamma_{0}\neq 0
whenever
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})
is sufficiently close to
\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)
in the space of bounded linear operators. To achieve this for all
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{Q}
with an
\epsilon
-independent constant
\delta_{Q}>0

we use the [Quadratic-ish Estimate] in Lemma
2.2
to estimate for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
and
(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}

\displaystyle\bigl\|\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})-\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\bigl\|\bigl(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\bigr)-\bigl(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\bigr)\bigr\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq 1}\bigl\|{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\leq c(\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})\;\leq\;\tfrac{1}{2}(C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q})^{-1}.

Here
c:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
is a monotone continuous function with
c(0)=0
that is a part of the data included in an adiabatic Fredholm family in Definition
2.1
. So we can choose
\delta_{Q}>0
so that
c(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}(C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q})^{-1}
for all
0\leq x\leq\delta_{Q}
.
This means that for each
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
the Fredholm map
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma,\epsilon}\to\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
fits into Lemma
4.4
, where the [Quadratic-ish Estimate] validates
(
28
) for
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}=0
with the function

\tilde{c}^{1}_{\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}}(0,\gamma_{0})=c(\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})
.
Thus the classical construction shows that

\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})={\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0})
is invertible for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
and
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{Q}
with inverses
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):=\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}
that satisfy the uniform bound
(
34
) with the constant
C_{Q}=2\,C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q}
.
We then replace this by the final constant
C_{Q}:=\max\{1,2\,C^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}_{Q}\}
to simplify later estimates in (
76
).

Next, we will assume [Uniform Continuity of
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
] as in Definition
2.13
and show that these inverses vary continuously with
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
– and that this continuity is locally uniform. This again follows by applying the classical proof in Lemma
4.4

for each
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
to the Fredholm map
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma,\epsilon}\to\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon}
, which now satisfies (
28
) for
\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
with the function
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})=c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\|\gamma^{\prime}_{0}-\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})
from Definition
2.13
.
Thus (
35
) follows directly from Lemma
4.4
.

Finally, suppose that the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing in the sense of Definition
2.8
,
which in particular means the implication

{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma\in\Omega\Rightarrow\gamma\in\Gamma
for
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
.
Then consider the value
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\mathfrak{k},\omega)
of
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})
at some
(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\in\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega
. This is the solution
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}
of

\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{k}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}=\omega.

In particular,
\gamma
solves
{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma=\mathfrak{c}+\omega\in\Omega
, which implies
\gamma\in\Gamma
by the regularizing property.
This shows that the restriction
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})|_{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}
for
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
takes values in
\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
. Then the injectivity and surjectivity of
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})
implies injectivity and surjectivity of the restriction, which establishes it as the claimed bijection
Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):=\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})|_{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}:\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega\to\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
. Moreover,
(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\mathfrak{k},\omega)
satisfies
\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{k}
and
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}=\omega
, thus
Q_{\epsilon}
is the inverse of
P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}):={\rm D}g_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0})
.
∎

4.2.
Contractions

The next step towards constructing finite dimensional reductions of adiabatic Fredholm families is to utilize the inverse operators from the previous section to rewrite the equations
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0
– up to a finite dimensional factor – as a fixed point problem for a family of contractions.
As in
[
HWZ21
, Def.3.1.11]
this will play the role of the Fredholm property in classical Fredholm descriptions of moduli spaces.

In the classical setting of Theorem
4.1
, this is based on showing that any
\mathcal{C}^{1}
Fredholm map satisfies the [Quadratic-ish Estimate] of Definition
2.1
– after shrinking its domain.

Lemma 4.6
.

Any Fredholm map
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to\Omega
as in Theorem
4.1

satisfies a
[Quadratic-ish Estimate]

(37)

\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma\bigr\|^{\Omega}\leq c(\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma})\|\gamma\|^{\overline{\Gamma}}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\overline{\Gamma}},\gamma\in\Gamma,

where
c:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
is a monotone continuous map with
c(0)=0

and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\subset\Gamma
is a convex open neighbourhood of
0
contained in
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
.
Further, let
Q(0):\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}
be the isomorphism from Lemma
4.4
, then the zero set
\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)\simeq\mathcal{G}_{Q}^{-1}(0)
is naturally identified with the zero set of the map

\mathcal{G}_{Q}\,:\;\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\;\rightarrow\;\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma},\hskip 20.00003pt(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\;\mapsto\;\bigl(\mathfrak{c},Q(0)(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\mathcal{F}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c})\bigr).

Moreover, this map is close to the identity map on

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}:=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\subset\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}=:\overline{W}

up to finite dimensional factors and a contraction in the following sense: We can write

\mathcal{G}_{Q}\,:\;\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\;\rightarrow\;\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{W},\hskip 20.00003pt(\mathfrak{k},w)\;\mapsto\;\bigl(A(\mathfrak{k},w),w-B(\mathfrak{k},w)\bigr),

where
A:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\mathfrak{C}
maps to the finite dimensional space
\mathfrak{C}
by projection
A(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=\mathfrak{c}
,
and
B:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\overline{W}
given by
B(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=Q(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{k},{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma-\mathcal{F}(\gamma)\bigr)
is a family of contractions near
0\in\overline{W}
parametrized by
\mathfrak{k}\in\mathfrak{K}
.
More precisely, given any
\theta\in(0,1)
there exists
\delta_{\theta}>0
such that for all
\mathfrak{k}\in\mathfrak{K},w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
we have

(38)

\displaystyle\|w\|^{W},\|w^{\prime}\|^{W}\leq\delta_{\theta}\hskip 10.00002pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\|B(\mathfrak{k},w^{\prime})-B(\mathfrak{k},w)\|^{W}\leq\theta\|w^{\prime}-w\|^{W}.

In addition, given any
\delta>0
we can find an open neighbourhood
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K},\delta}\subset\mathfrak{K}
of
0
with

(39)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K},\delta}\hskip 10.00002pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\|B(\mathfrak{k},0)\|^{W}<\delta.

If the map
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to\overline{\Omega}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
for some
\ell\geq 1
, then the contraction
B:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\overline{W}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
as well.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.4
we have

\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})\gamma-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(\gamma_{0})\gamma\bigr\|^{\Omega}\leq\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0})\|\gamma\|^{\overline{\Gamma}}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}},\gamma\in\Gamma,

where
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}\to[0,\infty)
is a continuous map that vanishes on the diagonal
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{0})=0
.
So to obtain (
37
) we are tempted to define
c:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
by
c^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}(x):=\sup_{\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\leq x}\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma_{0},0)
. While those supremums might not be finite for all
x>0
, the continuity
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma_{0},0)\to 0
as
\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}\to 0
guarantees
c^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}(x)\to 0
as
x\to 0
. Now pick
x_{0}>0
sufficiently small so that
c^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}(x_{0})<\infty
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\overline{\Gamma}}:=\{\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma\,|\,\|\gamma_{0}\|^{\Gamma}<x_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
.
Then we obtain the desired estimate by setting
c(x):=\min\{c^{\scriptscriptstyle prelim}(x),x_{0}\}
. This function is monotone by construction and inherits continuity from
\tilde{c}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Next, Lemma
4.2
identifies
\mathcal{F}^{-1}(0)\simeq\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)
for
\mathcal{G}:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega
given by
\mathcal{G}(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=(\mathfrak{c},\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\mathcal{F}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c})
.
The map considered here is obtained by composing
\mathcal{G}
with
{\rm Id}_{\mathfrak{C}}\times Q(0):\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega\to\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
. Here
Q(0):\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega\to\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
is a bijection by Lemma
4.4
, so this composition does not affect the zero set.

To check the expression for
B:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\overline{W}
given by
B(\mathfrak{k},w):=w-{\rm Pr}_{\overline{W}}(\mathcal{G}_{Q}(\mathfrak{k},w))
we first use the fact
Q(0)
is the inverse of the Fredholm stabilization isomorphism

P(0):\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}\to\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}
, given by

P(0)(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}(0)\gamma-\mathfrak{c})
in (
24
),
to rewrite

\displaystyle B(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)
