Contraction Property:

To check the contraction property (
43
) with
B:=\overline{B}_{\epsilon}
on
K:=\mathfrak{K}
and the subset
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}=\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
of
\overline{W}:=\overline{W}_{\epsilon}
equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|:=\|\cdot\|^{W}_{\epsilon}
for any
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
, we choose a fixed
\theta\in(0,1)
for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
in (
41
) to obtain a preliminary
\delta_{\sigma}:=\delta_{\theta}>0
with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\in\mathfrak{K},w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon},\|w\|^{W}_{\epsilon},\|w^{\prime}\|^{W}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{\sigma}\hskip 10.00002pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\|\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w^{\prime})-\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w)\|^{W}_{\epsilon}\leq\theta\|w^{\prime}-w\|^{W}_{\epsilon}.

Solution Maps:

Next, we choose a possibly smaller
0<\delta_{\sigma}\leq\min\{\delta_{\theta},\delta_{Q}\}
– with
\delta_{Q}>0
from Lemma
4.5
for later purposes – so that the above continues to hold along with the inclusion
\{w\in\overline{W}\,|\,\|w\|^{\overline{W}}\leq\delta_{\sigma}\}\subset\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
.
Now we use (
42
) with
\delta=(1-\theta)\delta_{\sigma}
to find neighbourhoods
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}:=\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K},\delta}\subset\mathfrak{K}
of
0
and
\Delta_{\sigma}:=\Delta_{\delta}\subset\Delta_{Q}
of
0
that guarantee (
44
) for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\hskip 10.00002pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\|\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},0)\|^{W}_{\epsilon}<(1-\theta)\delta_{\sigma}.

This confirms the assumptions of Lemma
4.9
for each
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
, which then provides unique solution maps
\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
such that

\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k},w)\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\,\big|\,w-\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w)=0,\|w\|^{W}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma}\bigr\}=\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))\,\big|\,\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\bigr\}.

Equivalently, these solutions
\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})=w_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}=(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
satisfy besides the smallness condition
\|w_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}\|^{W}_{\epsilon}=\|\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{\sigma}
the equations

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{k},{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(0)(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=\bigl(\mathfrak{k},{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}),{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}-\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}\bigr)=\bigl(\mathfrak{k},{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}-\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})\bigr)

(48)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=\mathfrak{k}\;\text{and}\;\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=0.

Here we used (
40
), (
33
), and the last version of the equation results from (
32
) for the map
\overline{g}
from Lemma
4.5
. This confirms the first part of the Lemma.

Uniform Bound and Uniform Continuity for Solution Maps:

The smallness condition for the solutions
\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})=(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})\|^{W}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{\sigma}
guarantees the claimed uniform bound on the solution maps with
c^{0}_{\sigma}\equiv 0
and
b^{0}_{\sigma}\equiv 1
.
Since
\delta_{\sigma}\leq\delta_{\theta}
for an a priori fixed
\theta\in(0,1)
this also guarantees the continuity estimate from (
45
), which further specifies with the help of (
34
) in Lemma
4.5
to
confirm [Uniform Continuity] of the solution maps: For any
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l}\in\mathfrak{K}
we have

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l})-\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\|\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l},\sigma(\mathfrak{k}))-\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\sigma(\mathfrak{k}))\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\bigl\|\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{l},{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}-\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})\bigr)-\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{k},{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}-\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})\bigr)\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

(49)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\bigl\|\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{l}-\mathfrak{k},0\bigr)\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}\;\leq\;\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\,C_{Q}\|\mathfrak{l}-\mathfrak{k}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}\;=:\;c^{0}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{l}-\mathfrak{k}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}).

Note that this uniform continuity of the solution maps did not require special regularity properties of the adiabatic Fredholm family. It is, however, also one of the two properties encoded in adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
regularity of the solution maps. Thus here is the explanation why the notion of adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
regularity of the Fredholm family in Definition
2.13
requires just one property (pointwise continuity), whereas
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity for
\ell\geq 1
requires two properties of the Fredholm family (pointwise and uniform continuity) – which will ensure the two properties (pointwise and uniform continuity) encoded in adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of the solution maps.

Fibrewise Regularity of Solution Maps:

It remains to establish the regularity of the solution maps – starting with the fibrewise regularity:
If the adiabatic Fredholm family is fibrewise
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular, that is
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}:(\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma},{\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}})\to(\Omega,\|\cdot\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon})
is uniformly
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
for each
\epsilon\in\Delta
, then the maps
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}:(\mathfrak{k},\gamma)\mapsto\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{k},{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma-\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)\bigr)
are uniformly
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
as well for each fixed
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
. Indeed,
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(0)
and
{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(0)
are linear operators and
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}
inherits its regularity from
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
by Lemma
2.4
.
In particular, the notion of an adiabatic Fredholm family in Definition
2.1
automatically includes fibrewise
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity, thus each contraction
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}
is continuous and
\mathcal{C}^{1}
for fixed
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
. When applying Lemma
4.9
to
B=\overline{B}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\to\overline{W}_{\epsilon}
this means, first, that
B
is continuous when
\mathcal{V}_{K}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
is equipped with the subspace topology of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathfrak{K}
. Then the Lemma asserts that each solution map
\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
is continuous.
Second, the
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity of each
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}
means that in Lemma
4.9
the contraction
B
is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
, and hence each solution map
\sigma_{\epsilon}
is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
.
Moreover, if the adiabatic Fredholm family is fibrewise
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular for some
\ell>1
in the sense of Definition
2.10
, then the maps
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}
are
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
by Lemma
2.2
, and hence each contraction
B=\overline{B}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\to\overline{W}_{\epsilon}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
. Then Lemma
4.9
guarantees that each solution map
\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\subset\overline{W}_{\epsilon}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
.

Restriction to the Regularizing Case:

Next, if the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing, then
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})=\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}\in\mathfrak{C}\subset\Omega
implies
\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\subset\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}
, so that each solution map takes values
\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{W},\mathfrak{k}\mapsto w_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}=(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}})
in the
\epsilon
-independent domain
\mathcal{V}_{W}=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
. This then allows us to rewrite the defining equation for the solution maps
\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})=\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))=B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))
in terms of the contractions
B_{\epsilon}:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{W}\to W
with
\epsilon
-independent domain and target spaces from Lemma
4.7
.

Adiabatic
\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^{0}}
Regularity:

Now assume in addition to the regularizing property that the adiabatic Fredholm family is adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
-regular as in Definition
2.13
, that is, given any
\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta
and a solution
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
of
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{0})\in\mathfrak{C}
, we have

\bigl\|\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})-\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\rightarrow 0
as
\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}
.
We already established uniform continuity in (
49
) above – which notably did not require any extra assumptions on the adiabatic Fredholm family.
So it remains to prove pointwise continuity in
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
of the solution maps for fixed
\mathfrak{k}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
. This can be estimated from the defining equation
\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})=B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))
and the contraction property (
41
)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

(50)

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))-B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\theta\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}.

Here we used the regularizing property to ensure
\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{W}=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
, so
that we can apply the
\|\cdot\|^{W}_{\epsilon}
norm to it.
This is also crucial to make sense of the expression
B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))\in W
. Without the regularizing property, we would need to make sense of “
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))
” where
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}
is defined on
\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
but
\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon_{0}}
. This type of triangle inequality computation will be used repeatedly in the following, and each time crucially relies on the regularizing property although we won’t keep pointing it out.

Less crucially, the regularizing property also simplifies the defining equation (
48
) for the solution map, where we specify to
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
and denote
(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}):=w_{0}:=\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})
,

(51)

\displaystyle w_{0}=B_{0}(\mathfrak{k},w_{0})\hskip 20.00003pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{0})=\mathfrak{k}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{0})=\mathfrak{c}_{0}

Now since
0<\theta<1
in the above estimate, we can absorb the first summand into the left hand side to establish [Pointwise Continuity in
\Delta
] of the solution map,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\bigl\|B_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}))-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

using (
40
) and the fact that
Q_{\epsilon}(0)P_{\epsilon}(0)={\rm Id}_{\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma}
from Lemma
4.5

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(0)\bigl(\mathfrak{k}_{0},{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{0}-\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\bigr)-Q_{\epsilon}(0)P_{\epsilon}(0)(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(0)\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\overline{W}_{\epsilon})}\bigl\|\bigl(\mathfrak{k}_{0},{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{0}-\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\bigr)-\bigl(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{0}),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(0)\gamma_{0}-\mathfrak{c}_{0}\bigr)\bigr\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}

using (
34
) in Lemma
4.5

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\,C_{Q}\bigl(\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}-\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{0})\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+\bigl\|\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})-\mathfrak{c}_{0}\bigr\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr)

using (
51
)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\theta}\,C_{Q}\bigl(\|0\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+\bigl\|\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})-\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{0})\bigr\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr)\hskip 10.00002pt\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\hskip 10.00002pt0.

Here the final convergence holds by adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
regularity of the adiabatic Fredholm family in Definition
2.13
at the solution
\gamma_{0}
of the
\epsilon_{0}
-equation modulo cokernel
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{0})=\mathfrak{c}_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}
.
Now continuity of the family of solution maps
\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\bigl(\mathcal{V}_{W},\|\cdot\|^{W}_{0}\bigr),(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\mapsto\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})
can be deduced by combining [Lower Bound on Norms] with [Pointwise Continuity in
\Delta
] and the previously established [Uniform Continuity] in (
49
):
For any
\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\mathfrak{k}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
we obtain for
\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\ni(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\to(\epsilon_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})

\displaystyle\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

(52)

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})-\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c^{0}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}-\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{\mathfrak{K}})+\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\underset{(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\to(\epsilon_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})}{\longrightarrow}\hskip 10.00002pt0.

Overview of Adiabatic
\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^{\ell}}
Regularity:

Finally, we assume that the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing and adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular for some
\ell\geq 1
as in Definition
2.13
. Then we established above that each solution map
\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{W}
takes values in the
\epsilon
-independent subspace
\mathcal{V}_{W}\subset W
and is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
with respect to the
\epsilon
-dependent norm
\|\cdot\|^{W}_{\epsilon}
that gives rise to the ambient Banach space
\mathcal{V}_{W}\subset\overline{W}_{\epsilon}
.
The assumption of adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity in Definition
2.13
guarantees the higher order regularizing property

\underline{\gamma}\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon},\;{\rm T}^{\ell}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\Omega\hskip 10.00002pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\underline{\gamma}\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}

and it guarantees two types of regularity for varying
\epsilon\in\Delta
:

[Pointwise Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm T}}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
in
\Delta
at solutions modulo
\mathbf{\mathfrak{C}}
]

Given any
\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta
and a solution
\underline{\gamma}_{0}\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
of the linearized equation modulo cokernel
{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{0})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}
, we have

(53)

\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Omega}_{\epsilon}\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0.

[Uniform Continuity of
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
]

There is a monotone continuous function
c^{\ell}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}:=c^{\ell,\delta_{Q}}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
with
c^{\ell}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}(0)=0
so that for all
\epsilon\in\Delta
and
\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}},\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}}\in{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
with
\|\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\|\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{Q}

we have (via Remark
2.14
for
\delta=\delta_{Q}
)

(54)

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}})-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}({\rm T}^{\ell-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon},{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon})}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\leq c^{\ell}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}(\|\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\Gamma}_{\epsilon})\,\max\bigl\{1,\|\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{l}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}\Gamma}_{\epsilon},\|\underline{\gamma}^{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr\}^{\ell}.

Now our goal is to show that the family of solution maps
\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{W}\bigr)_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}
is adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
in the sense that it satisfies the analogous types of regularity for varying
\epsilon\in\Delta
:

[Pointwise Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm T}}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
]

Given any
\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
we have

(55)

\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0.

[Uniform Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm T}}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
]

There are monotone continuous functions
c^{\ell}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

and
b^{\ell}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)
with
c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(0)=0

so that for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}},\underline{\mathfrak{l}}\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
we have

(56)

\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}\leq c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\}).

Before proving these two continuity properties we will show that they imply the remaining claims.

[Uniform Bound]

follows from combining the [Uniform Continuity of
{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
] with the fact that

{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0},0,\ldots,0)=(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0}),0,\ldots,0)
, and thus for any
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}=(\mathfrak{k}^{0},\mathfrak{k}^{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{k}^{N_{\ell}})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0},\mathfrak{k}^{1}\ldots\mathfrak{k}^{N_{\ell}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0},0\ldots 0)\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0},0\ldots 0)\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|(0,\mathfrak{k}^{1}\ldots\mathfrak{k}^{N_{\ell}})\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|(\mathfrak{k}^{0},0\ldots 0)\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\})+\bigl\|(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0}),0\ldots 0)\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})+\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}^{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})+\delta_{\sigma}.

[Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm T}}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
w.r.t.
\mathbf{\|\cdot\|_{0}}
]

follows by combining
[Pointwise Continuity] and [Uniform Continuity] with [Lower Bound on Norms]:
The map

\Delta_{\sigma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{W},\|\cdot\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{0}\bigr),(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\mapsto{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})
is continuous since for any
\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
we have for
\Delta_{\sigma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\ni(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\to(\epsilon_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

(57)

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\})+\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\underset{(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\to(\epsilon_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})}{\longrightarrow}\hskip 10.00002pt0.\phantom{M^{N}}

Here we used the fact that
c^{\ell}_{\sigma}
is continuous with
c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(0)=0
and that

\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\to\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}
as
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\to\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}
, so that

b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\})
stays bounded by continuity of
b^{\ell}_{\sigma}

So it remains to prove the two properties of adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity for the solution maps – which we will do by induction in
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. The induction step – below after (
63
) – can be interpreted to work with
\ell=0
as the base case (which is established above), but to improve accessibility of the argument, we first go through the computations for the case
\ell=1
.

Induction Base Case – Adiabatic
\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^{1}}
Regularity

To begin this proof we use the already established differentiability for fixed
\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}
to compute the derivative of the solution maps at
\mathfrak{k}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
from the defining equation (
48
) in terms of the family of maps
\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}}:(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\mapsto(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{k},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)-\mathfrak{c})
from Lemma
4.5
. Here we denote
\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=:(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})
to compute for any
\mathfrak{k}_{1}\in\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}+\lambda\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}+\lambda\mathfrak{k}_{1}))=0

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Rightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\lambda}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}+\lambda\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}+\lambda\mathfrak{k}_{1}))\big|_{\lambda=0}\;=\;{\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})){\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}+\tfrac{\partial}{\partial\mathfrak{k}}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))\mathfrak{k}_{1}\;=\;0

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon}){\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}+(-\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)=(0,0)

(58)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon}){\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}=(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)\;,\hskip 20.00003pt\text{where}\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})=\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}=\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0).

Thus
{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}):{\rm T}_{\mathfrak{k}_{0}}\mathfrak{K}=\mathfrak{K}\to{\rm T}_{\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})}W=W
is the composition of the inclusion
\mathfrak{K}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{K}\times\Gamma
with the inverse
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})={\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}))^{-1}:\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega\to\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma=W
of
{\rm D}\overline{g}_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})
. Here the inverse
\overline{Q}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})
exists – and varies continuously with
\gamma_{\epsilon}
– since we have taken care to construct the solution map so that

\|\gamma_{\epsilon}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\leq\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})\|^{W}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma}\leq\delta_{Q}
guarantees applicability of Lemma
4.5
.

[Regularizing Property of
\mathbf{{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}}
]

Recall that
\mathcal{C}^{1}
adiabatic regularity includes two regularizing properties in Definition
2.8
.
The first was used to establish the solution maps as maps between the
\epsilon
-independent dense subspaces
\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{W}
. Now given
(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})\in{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
and denoting

\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=:(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})
and
(\mathfrak{c},\zeta):={\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}\in\overline{W}_{\epsilon}=\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}
, the above defining equation together with (
33
) yields

\displaystyle\overline{P}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{c},\zeta)+(-\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)=(0,0)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\zeta)-\mathfrak{k}_{1}=0,\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\zeta+\mathfrak{c}=0

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Rightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm D}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\zeta=-\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{C}\hskip 10.00002pt\Rightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\zeta\in\Gamma,

where we used the second (linearized) regularizing property (
9
) in Definition
2.8
.
With Remark
2.9
this establishes the tangent solution maps as maps between the
\epsilon
-independent dense subspaces
{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}:{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{W}
.

[Pointwise Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
]

will be proved by fixing
\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta_{\sigma}
,
\mathfrak{k}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
\mathfrak{k}_{1}\in\mathfrak{K}
, and writing

{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})=(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1})

where

\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=:(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})
solves (
48
) for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
.
To establish pointwise continuity

\bigl\|{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})-{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}W}_{\epsilon}\to 0
as
\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}
as in (
55
) note that we already showed

\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\|^{W}_{\epsilon}=\|\gamma_{\epsilon}-\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}+\|\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon}-\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon_{0}}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0,

so it remains to prove
\bigl\|{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}-{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}\to 0
as
\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}
. Note here that applying the
\|\cdot\|^{W}_{\epsilon}
norm to this difference only makes sense because we already established that the differentials take values in the
\epsilon
-independent space
W
.
Thus
{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}=:(\mathfrak{c}_{1},\gamma_{1})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma

is the solution of

(59)

\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{1})=\mathfrak{k}_{1}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}})\gamma_{1}=\mathfrak{c}_{1}.

Here we used the explicit form of
{\rm D}g_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon}):(\mathfrak{k},\gamma)\mapsto(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}},{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\gamma-\mathfrak{c})
from Lemma
4.5
.
Thus
(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}},\gamma_{1})\in{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
is a solution of the linearized equation modulo cokernel

{\rm T}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}},\gamma_{1})\;=\;\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}}),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}})\gamma_{1}\bigr)\;=\;\bigl(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon_{0}},\mathfrak{c}_{1}\bigr)\;\in\;{\rm T}\mathfrak{C}

which will allow us to apply (
53
) below.
Now for
\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}\in\Delta_{\sigma}
we can use the fact that
Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon}){\rm D}g_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})={\rm Id}_{\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma}
from Lemma
4.5
to estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}-{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)-Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon}){\rm D}g_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{c}_{1},\gamma_{1})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},\overline{W}_{\epsilon})}\bigl(\bigl\|\mathfrak{k}_{1}-\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma_{1})\bigr\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+\bigl\|-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\gamma_{1}+\mathfrak{c}_{1}\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)

using (
34
) and (
59
)

\displaystyle\leq C_{Q}\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\gamma_{1}-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}})\gamma_{1}\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C_{Q}\bigl(\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\gamma_{1}-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}})\gamma_{1}\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}})\gamma_{1}-{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}})\gamma_{1}\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)

using (
54
) and (
53
) for
{\rm T}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})=(\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{1})

\displaystyle\leq C_{Q}\bigl(c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\|\gamma_{\epsilon}-\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})+\bigl\|{\rm T}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}},\gamma_{1})-{\rm T}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\gamma_{\epsilon_{0}},\gamma_{1})\bigr\|^{\Omega}_{\epsilon}\bigr)\hskip 10.00002pt\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\hskip 10.00002pt0.

[Uniform Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}}
]

follows similarly from the previously established
uniform continuity of
\sigma_{\epsilon}
together with uniform continuity of
{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}
.
To establish the latter, we consider
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}=(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1}),\underline{\mathfrak{l}}=(\mathfrak{l}_{0},\mathfrak{l}_{1})\in{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
and write

\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=:(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})
resp.
\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l}_{0})=:(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{\epsilon},\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})

to estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l}_{0})\,\mathfrak{l}_{1}-{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\,\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{l}_{1},0)-Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{l}_{1},0)-Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)-Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},\overline{W}_{\epsilon})}\|\mathfrak{l}_{1}-\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon})-Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}(\mathfrak{K}\times\overline{\Omega}_{\epsilon},\overline{W}_{\epsilon})}\|\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

using (
34
) and (
35
) in Lemma
4.5

\displaystyle\leq C_{Q}\|\mathfrak{l}_{1}-\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+(C_{Q})^{2}\,c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\|\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon}-\gamma_{\epsilon}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon})\|\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}.

Combining this with the already established uniform continuity estimate (
49
) for
\sigma_{\epsilon}
yields

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}})-{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l}_{0})-\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l}_{0})\,\mathfrak{l}_{1}-{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\,\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr\|^{W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}\|\mathfrak{l}_{0}-\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+C_{Q}\|\mathfrak{l}_{1}-\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}+(C_{Q})^{2}\,c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl(\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}\,\|\mathfrak{l}_{0}-\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\|\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}\|(\mathfrak{l}_{0},\mathfrak{l}_{1})-(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})\|^{{\rm T}\mathfrak{K}}+(C_{Q})^{2}\,c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl(\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}\,\|\mathfrak{l}_{0}-\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\max\{1,\|\mathfrak{l}_{1}\|^{\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}_{1}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}\mathfrak{K}}+(C_{Q})^{2}\,c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl(\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}\,\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\max\{1,\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{K}},\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{K}}\}

\displaystyle\leq c^{1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{1}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{K}}\})

with

c^{1}_{\sigma}(x):=\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}x+(C_{Q})^{2}\,c^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\tfrac{C_{Q}}{1-\theta}x)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003ptb^{1}_{\sigma}(x):=\max\{1,x\}.

This finishes the proof that adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity of the adiabatic Fredholm family implies adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{1}
regularity of the solution maps.

Induction Step – Adiabatic
\mathbf{\mathcal{C}^{\ell+1}}
Regularity

Now suppose we proved that adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of an adiabatic Fredholm family as in Definition
2.13
implies adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of the solution maps and uniform bounds (
47
) for some
\ell\geq 1
19
19

19

To make sense of this proof with induction base case
\ell=0
, ignore intermediate arguments involving
{\rm T}^{0-1}
of any map
f
, and interpret
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{0-1}f(v)=f
.

and consider an adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell+1}
-regular adiabatic Fredholm family
\bigl((\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\to\Omega)_{\epsilon\in\Delta},\ldots\bigr)
and its solution maps
\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{W}\bigr)_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}
as constructed above.
Then the
(\ell+1)
-st tangent of the solution maps

\displaystyle{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon}={\rm T}\,{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}:\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}={\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm T}^{\ell+1}W={\rm T}^{\ell}W\times{\rm T}^{\ell}W

\displaystyle\bigl(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longmapsto\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}),{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr)

are pointwise continuous in
\Delta
and uniformly continuous in the first component
{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
by the induction hypothesis.
So to prove that
\sigma_{\epsilon}
is adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell+1}
-regular it remains to consider the second component
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
and prove its pointwise and uniform continuity. This is classically achieved by casting the linearizations of the fixed point problems with solutions
\sigma_{\epsilon}
as higher order fixed point problems whose solutions are the derivatives under consideration. That leads to the same estimates as the following approach in which we generalize the explicit expression for the differential

{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}=Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)
when
\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon})
.
To generalize this expression for the first differential of the solution maps we rewrite the derivative of the defining equation (
58
) for the solution maps more concisely as the identity

(60)

\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}\circ{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}

for the maps with abbreviation
V:=\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{V}_{W}\times V,

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),\mathfrak{k}_{1},0\bigr),

\displaystyle{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle\to{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{W}=\mathcal{V}_{W}\times W,

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr),

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{W}=\mathcal{V}_{W}\times W

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{V}_{W}\times V,

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(w_{0}=(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}),w_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(w_{0},P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\,w_{1}\bigr).

Indeed, applying the left hand side of (
60
) to any
(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})\in{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{k}}
yields as claimed

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}\bigl({\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\,\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr)\;=\;\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})=(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon},\gamma_{\epsilon}),P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{\epsilon})\,{\rm D}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})\,\mathfrak{k}_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:D defining}}{=}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),(\mathfrak{k}_{1},0)\bigr)\;=\;\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1}).

Since
P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c})
is given by (
33
), the identity (
60
) also encodes the fact that the tangent map of the solution map solves the tangent version of the stablized equation
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)\in\mathfrak{C}
,

(\mathfrak{c},\gamma,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\xi)={\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})\hskip 20.00003pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt{\rm T}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma,\xi)=(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})\in{\rm T}\mathfrak{C},

where
{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{W}={\rm T}(\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma})=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
.
Identifying
{\rm T}^{\ell}{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{W}\simeq{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\Gamma

and taking tangent maps of the above implication yields the higher tangent stabilized equation

(61)

\bigl(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime},\underline{\xi}\bigr)\;=\;{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1})\hskip 20.00003pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 20.00003pt{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{\xi})=(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{C}.

[Higher Regularizing Property]:

More precisely, the higher tangent solution maps
{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}:{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
inititially exist as maps to the completion by Lemma
4.9
, so that we obtain the above conclusion in terms of the extended Fredholm map

{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{\xi})=(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{C}
.
However, the higher regularizing property (
11
) in Definition
2.13

then implies
(\underline{\gamma},\underline{\xi})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
and hence

{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{\xi})={\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{\xi})
.
This also shows that the higher tangent solution maps

{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}:{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{W}

take values in the
\epsilon
-independent dense subspace.

Furthermore, the map
\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}
is invertible by Lemma
4.5
, with the inverse explicitly given by

(62)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{W}\times V

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{V}_{W}\times W={\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{W},

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt(w_{0}=(\mathfrak{c}_{0},\gamma_{0}),\mathfrak{k},\omega)

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(w_{0},Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\mathfrak{k},\omega)\bigr).

With that notation we can rewrite (
60
) equivalently as

(63)

{\rm T}\sigma_{\epsilon}=\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}\circ\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}.

Note here that the maps
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
have the same regularity as
\sigma_{\epsilon}
, and the maps

\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}
have the same regularity as
{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
since

P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(w=(\mathfrak{c},\gamma))=\bigl(\pi_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma-\mathfrak{c}\bigr)
.
Towards analyzing higher order derivatives, we apply the tangent map construction and chain rule (
5
) repeatedly to (
60
) to obtain

(64)

{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}\circ{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}={\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}.

Analogously, (
63
) yields

{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}={\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}\circ{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
,
and taking the differential of this identity yields

(65)

{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})={\rm D}\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}\circ{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\bigr)(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})={\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}({\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}))\,\circ\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}).

This is an inductively explicit formula for
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
since the
\ell
-th order derivatives of
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
on the right hand side are determined by
\ell
-th order derivatives of
\sigma_{\epsilon}
. And this inductive formula will serve to prove adiabatic regularity of
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
since the
\ell
-th order derivative of
\sigma_{\epsilon}
is adiabatic regular by induction hypothesis, and the derivatives of the inverse operators in
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
can be estimated in terms of
(\ell+1)
-st order derivatives of the adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell+1}
-regular adiabatic Fredholm family
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
.
We will establish these estimates in two preliminary steps before proving the two adiabatic regularity properties (pointwise and uniform continuity) of
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
.

Controlling derivatives of
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
by derivatives of
\sigma_{\epsilon}
:

The maps
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
naturally split

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\;\simeq\;\sigma_{\epsilon}\times I_{\mathfrak{K}}\;:\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{V}_{W}\times V

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{W}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longmapsto\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\mathfrak{k}_{1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}),\mathfrak{k}_{1},0\bigr)

into the original solution maps
\sigma_{\epsilon}
and the
\epsilon
-independent inclusion
I_{\mathfrak{K}}:\mathfrak{K}\to V=\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega,\mathfrak{k}\mapsto(\mathfrak{k},0)
.
We will identify tangent maps analogously, by splitting higher tangent spaces according to

{\rm T}{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\;=\bigcup_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}}{\rm T}_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})}{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\;=\bigcup_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathfrak{K}}{\rm T}_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})}\bigl(\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{K}\bigr)\;\simeq\bigcup_{\mathfrak{v}_{0}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}}{\rm T}_{\mathfrak{v}_{0}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\bigcup_{\mathfrak{k}_{0}\in\mathfrak{K}}{\rm T}_{\mathfrak{k}_{0}}\mathfrak{K}

rather than the usual

{\rm T}{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\;=\bigcup_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}}{\rm T}_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})}{\rm T}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\;=\bigcup_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathfrak{K}}{\rm T}_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})}\bigl(\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{K}\bigr)\;=\bigcup_{(\mathfrak{v}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{0})\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathfrak{K}}{\rm T}_{\mathfrak{v}_{0}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}_{\mathfrak{k}_{0}}\mathfrak{K}.

Thus for all
k\geq 1
we identify
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}=(\mathfrak{k}^{0},\mathfrak{k}^{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{k}^{N_{k}})\in{\rm T}^{k}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
for
N_{k}=2^{k}-1
with

(66)

\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\;\simeq\;(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\,:=\;\bigl((\mathfrak{k}^{0},\mathfrak{k}^{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{k}^{N_{k}-1}),(\mathfrak{k}^{1},\mathfrak{k}^{3},\ldots,\mathfrak{k}^{N_{k}})\bigr)\;\in\;{\rm T}^{k-1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{k-1}\mathfrak{K}

when writing

\displaystyle{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}\;\simeq\;{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}\;:\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{W}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}V

\displaystyle(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\;

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longmapsto\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}),{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr).

Here the inclusion map
I_{\mathfrak{K}}
is linear, hence
{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}
is given by
I_{\mathfrak{K}}
in each component, which is naturally bounded
\|I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\mathfrak{k})\|^{V}_{\epsilon}=\|(\mathfrak{k},0)\|^{\mathfrak{K}\times\Omega}_{\epsilon}=\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{\mathfrak{K}}
.
Now we can transfer the uniform bounds and continuity estimates from
{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
to
{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
as follows:
First, the inductive assumption (
47
) implies the uniform bound for any
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1})=\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\simeq(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}\simeq{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\;=\;\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

(67)

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}V}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\sigma}+c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})+\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\sigma}+\bigl(c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})+\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})

with
\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x):=c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)+x
a new monotone, continuous function with
\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(0)=0
.

Second, for fixed
(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1})=\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\simeq(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}
we write

{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od}):={\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}
.
Then for
\Delta_{\sigma}\ni\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}
the pointwise continuity of
{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
given by the inductive assumption in (
55
) implies the pointwise continuity

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}),{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr)-\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}),{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr)\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

(68)

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}\;\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\;0.

Third, for any
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\simeq(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od}),\underline{\mathfrak{l}}\simeq(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev},\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}
the inductive assumption in (
56
) implies the uniform continuity

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}),{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr)-\bigl({\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}),{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr)\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})-{\rm T}^{\ell}I_{\mathfrak{K}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}V}_{\epsilon}

(69)

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle ev}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\})+\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle od}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})+\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\})

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\}).

These three implications of the inductive assumption will be crucial for the adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell+1}
regularity. Before proving it, we will establish one more set of estimates in preparation.

Controlling derivatives of
\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
by derivatives of
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
:

The final preparation step is to establish uniform continuity and bounds for the derivatives of the map
\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
given by (
62
). For that purpose we will go back to its expression in components
20
20

20

Alternatively, the chain rule applied to the identity
\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}\circ\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}={\rm Id}_{W\times W}
allows one to express
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
as an inverse of
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}
. However, while the latter is uniformly continuous and invertible, we couldn’t find an explicit formula for this inverse that was suitable for transferring the uniform continuity estimates to
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{W}\times V

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{V}_{W}\times W,

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt(w=(\mathfrak{c},\gamma),v)

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(w,Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma)v\bigr)

and use the product rule in the identity
Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma)\,P_{\epsilon}(\gamma)={\rm Id}_{W}
from Lemma
4.5
.
More precisely, our goal is to prove uniform bounds and continuity of
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
.
So we begin by analyzing the higher tangent maps
{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
defined in Definition
2.5
,

\displaystyle{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(\mathcal{V}_{W}\times V)\simeq{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}V

\displaystyle\;\to\;{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}W\simeq{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(\mathcal{V}_{W}\times W),

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})

\displaystyle\;\mapsto\;\bigl(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{w}:={\Pi}_{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}W}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})\bigr),

where the
N_{\ell-1}=2^{\ell-1}-1
components of
\underline{w}=(w_{0},\ldots,w_{N_{\ell}})
are functions of
\underline{\gamma}=(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{N_{\ell-1}})
and
\underline{v}=(v_{0},\ldots,v_{N_{\ell-1}})
starting with

\displaystyle w_{0}

\displaystyle=Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})v_{0}

\displaystyle w_{1}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}|_{t=0}\bigl(Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0}+t\gamma_{1})(v_{0}+tv_{1})\bigr)\;=\;Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})v_{1}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{1}]v_{0}.

Next,
(w_{2},w_{3})
arise from the differential
{\rm D}(w_{0},w_{1})
at base point
(\gamma_{0},v_{0})
applied to
(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},v_{2},v_{3})
,

\displaystyle w_{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}|_{t=0}w_{0}\bigl((\gamma_{0},v_{0})+t(\gamma_{2},v_{2})\bigr)\;=\;Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})v_{2}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{2}]v_{0}

\displaystyle w_{3}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}|_{t=0}w_{1}\bigl((\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},v_{0},v_{1})+t(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},v_{2},v_{3})\bigr)

\displaystyle=Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})v_{3}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{2}]v_{1}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{1}]v_{2}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{3}]v_{0}+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})]v_{0}.

\displaystyle w_{4}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle w_{7}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}|_{t=0}w_{3}\bigl((\gamma_{0},v_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{3},v_{3})+t(\gamma_{4},v_{4},\ldots,\gamma_{7},v_{7})\bigr)

\displaystyle=Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})v_{7}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{4}]v_{3}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{2}]v_{5}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{6}]v_{1}+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{4})]v_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{1}]v_{6}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{5}]v_{2}+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{4})]v_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{3}]v_{4}+[{\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\gamma_{7}]v_{0}+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{3},\gamma_{4})]v_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})]v_{4}+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{5},\gamma_{2})]v_{0}+[{\rm D}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{6})]v_{0}+[{\rm D}^{3}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{4})]v_{0}

Continuing inductively, each component of
{\Pi}_{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}W}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})
is a sum of expressions
[{\rm D}^{k}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{*},\ldots,\gamma_{*})]v_{*}

of the forms

\displaystyle O^{k}_{1,*}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})\,:\;\bigl(\underline{\xi}=(\xi_{0},\ldots),\underline{y}=(y_{0},\ldots)\bigr)

(70)

\displaystyle O^{k}_{2,*}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})\,:\;\bigl(\underline{\xi}=(\xi_{0},\ldots),\underline{y}=(y_{0},\ldots)\bigr)

\displaystyle\mapsto[{\rm D}^{k}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{*},\ldots,\xi_{*},\ldots,\gamma_{*})]v_{*}

\displaystyle O^{k}_{3,*}(\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})\,:\;\bigl(\underline{\xi}=(\xi_{0},\ldots),\underline{y}=(y_{0},\ldots)\bigr)

\displaystyle\mapsto[{\rm D}^{k}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{*},\ldots,\gamma_{*})]y_{*}.

Consequently, the differential of
{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}
at a base point

\displaystyle{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma},\underline{v})

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}V

\displaystyle\;\to\;{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}W,

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt(\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime},\underline{\xi},\underline{y})

\displaystyle 0={\rm D}\bigl[Q_{\epsilon}\,P_{\epsilon}\bigr](\gamma_{0})\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle={\rm D}Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1})\,P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})+Q_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})\,{\rm D}P_{\epsilon}(\gamma_{0})(\gamma_{1})
