(\underline{\xi}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{0})\,:=\;{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\;\in\;{\rm T}_{\underline{w}_{\epsilon_{0}}}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{W}\;\simeq\;{\rm T}_{\underline{w}_{\epsilon}}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{W}\;\simeq\;{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{C}.

Using the above identifications we can bound this difference by

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl({\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{w}_{\epsilon_{0}})-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{w}_{\epsilon})\bigr)(\underline{\xi}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\bigl({\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}-{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\Pi\,\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{0}\bigr)-\bigl({\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}-{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\Pi\,\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{0}\bigr)\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}{\rm D}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\bigl({\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon})-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}})\bigr)\,\underline{\xi}_{0}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Omega}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}})\,\underline{\xi}_{0}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Omega}_{\epsilon}

using (
54
) with
\ell
replaced by
\ell+1

(83)

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell+1}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}(\|\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon}-\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Gamma}_{\epsilon})\,\|\underline{\xi}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Gamma}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\xi}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\xi}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\Omega}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0.

To deduce the claimed convergence note that
\underline{\xi}_{0}
is a fixed vector,
c^{\ell+1}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}
is continuous with
c^{\ell+1}_{{\rm T}\mathcal{F}}(0)=0
, and by the induction hypothesis

\bigl\|\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon}-\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\;\leq\;\bigl\|(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{\epsilon})-(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{\epsilon_{0}})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}\;=\;\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}\;\underset{\epsilon\to\epsilon_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\;0.

For the second term in (
83
), convergence is guaranteed by
[Continuity of
{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
in
\Delta
rel.
\mathbf{\mathfrak{C}}
]
since
(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\xi}_{0})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
is a part of the vector

\bigl(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\xi}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{0}\bigr)={\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon_{0}}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1})
which solves the stabilized equation

{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\gamma}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\xi}_{0})=(\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{\epsilon_{0}},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}_{0})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{C}

by (
61
).
This establishes pointwise continuity of the
(\ell+1)
-st tangent of the solution maps

{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon}=({\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon},{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon})
. To establish adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell+1}
regularity of the solution maps as in Definition
2.13
, it remains to prove the following.

Claim: [Uniform Continuity of
\mathbf{{\rm D}}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
]

There are monotone continuous functions
\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

and
\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)

with
\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(0)=0
so that for all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}

and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}=(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}),\underline{\mathfrak{l}}=(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}={\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}

we have

\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}\leq\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\}).

Once established, this claim combines with the induction hypothesis (
56
) to prove [Uniform Continuity of
{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon}
]: For all
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}

and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}=(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}),\underline{\mathfrak{l}}=(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1})\in{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1})-{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}+\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\})+\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})+\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\bigr)\,\max\{b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\}),\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\})

\displaystyle\leq c^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\})

where
c^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x):=c^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)+\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x)

and
b^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x):=\max\{b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x),\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x)\}

defines the required functions
c^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

and
b^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)
,
which inherit monotonicity, continuity, and the value
c^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(0)=0
from their constituents.

To prove the claim we consider
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}=(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}),\underline{\mathfrak{l}}=(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1})\in{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}
and denote

\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}}\,:=\;{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\;=\;(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}},\ldots)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003pt\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}\,:=\;{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0});=\;(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}},\gamma_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}},\ldots),

where
(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}},\gamma_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})=\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}^{0})
and
(\mathfrak{c}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}},\gamma_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})=\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{0})
are the solution maps applied to the base points of
\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}=(\mathfrak{k}_{0}^{0},\ldots)
and
\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}=(\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{0},\ldots)
.
Then we can use (
67
) to estimate the scaling factor as a function of

x=\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\}
by

\displaystyle M(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}},\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})

\displaystyle=\max\{1,\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon},\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\max\{1,\|{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon},\|{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\bigr\}

(84)

\displaystyle\leq\max\{1,\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{l}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{K}}),\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}\mathfrak{K}})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\{1,\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\,b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr\}.

With that we can finally estimate the continuity of
{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
by

\displaystyle\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})\,{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\bigr\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}({\rm T}^{\ell-1}(\overline{W}_{\epsilon}\times\overline{V}_{\epsilon}),{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(\overline{W}_{\epsilon}\times\overline{W}_{\epsilon}))}\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}})-{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{Q}_{\epsilon}(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})\bigr\|^{\mathcal{L}({\rm T}^{\ell-1}(\overline{W}_{\epsilon}\times\overline{V}_{\epsilon}),{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(\overline{W}_{\epsilon}\times\overline{W}_{\epsilon}))}\bigl\|{\rm D}{\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})\,\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{1}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

using (
78
) and (
79
)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\,\max\{1,\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\}^{\ell}\cdot\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}})-{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\bigl(c^{\leq\ell}_{{\rm D}Q}\bigl(\|\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{\epsilon}-\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{\epsilon}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr)+\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\bigr)\,M(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}},\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})^{\ell+1}\cdot\bigl\|{\rm T}^{\ell}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}

using (
69
) and (
67
)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\,\max\{1,\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\}^{\ell}\cdot\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\,M(\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}},\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}})^{\ell}\bigl(c^{\leq\ell}_{{\rm D}Q}\bigl(\|\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{\epsilon}-\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{\epsilon}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr)+\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\bigr)

using (
84
) with
x:=\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\,\max\{1,\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\,b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr\}^{\ell}\Bigl(b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\,\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\bigl(\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr)\,\bigl(c^{\leq\ell}_{{\rm D}Q}\bigl(\|\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{\epsilon}-\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{\epsilon}\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr)+\|\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell-1}(W\times V)}_{\epsilon}\bigr)\Bigr)

using (
69
) for

\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{l}}={\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}),\underline{z}_{\epsilon}^{\mathfrak{k}}={\rm T}^{\ell-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0})
and
(
49
) for
\gamma^{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}=\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}^{0}),\gamma^{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}=\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{0})

\displaystyle\leq C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\max\bigl\{1,\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr\}^{\ell}\,\max\bigl\{b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x),\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\Bigl(\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})+c^{\leq\ell}_{{\rm D}Q}\bigl(c^{0}_{\sigma}(\|\mathfrak{l}^{0}_{0}-\mathfrak{k}^{0}_{0}\|^{\mathfrak{K}})\bigr)+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}\bigl(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}_{0}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\,b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}_{0}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell}\mathfrak{K}}\})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\max\bigl\{1,\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr\}^{\ell}\,\max\bigl\{b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x),b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)(\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x))\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\Bigl(\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})+c^{\leq\ell}_{{\rm D}Q}\bigl(c^{0}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}})\bigr)+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}\bigl(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}\bigl(\max\{\|\mathfrak{l}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}},\|\mathfrak{k}\|^{{\rm T}_{\bullet}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}\}\bigr)\,\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(\|\underline{\mathfrak{l}}-\underline{\mathfrak{k}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell+1}\mathfrak{K}}),

where the functions
\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)
and
\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
are given by

\displaystyle\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x)

\displaystyle:=C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\max\bigl\{1,\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr\}^{\ell}\,\max\bigl\{b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x),b^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x)(\delta_{\sigma}+\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)\,b^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x))\bigr\}

\displaystyle\text{and}\hskip 20.00003pt\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x)

\displaystyle:=\tilde{c}^{\ell}_{\sigma}(x)+c^{\leq\ell}_{{\rm D}Q}\bigl(c^{0}_{\sigma}(x)\bigr)+\tilde{c}^{\ell-1}_{\sigma}(x),

and inherit monotonicity, continuity, and the value
\hat{c}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(0)=0
from their constituents.
In particular,
\hat{b}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}(x)\geq 1
is guaranteed by
C^{\ell}_{{\rm T}Q}\geq 1
in (
78
).
This finishes the last claim in the inductive step and thus proves for any
\ell\geq 1
that adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of the adiabatic Fredholm family implies adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of the solution map.
∎

4.4.
Finite Dimensional Reduction

This section finishes the proof of Theorem
1.8
in Corollary
4.12
by inserting the solution maps from §
4.3
back into the remaining finite dimensional equations identified in §
4.2
. More precisely – in the regularizing case – the two components of the solution map
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\mapsto\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\Gamma
play different roles:
The
\mathfrak{C}
component gives rise to the finite dimensional equation
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\mapsto{\rm Pr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))
, whereas the
\Gamma
component yields the map
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\mapsto\bigl(\epsilon,{\rm Pr}_{\Gamma}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))\bigr)
to the solution spaces
\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
.

Starting again in the classical Fredholm setting of Theorem
4.1
, we work here directly with the form of the map
\mathcal{G}_{Q}
that was constructed in Lemma
4.6
. Then this Lemma finishes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Consider a Banach space
(\overline{W},\|\cdot\|)
, an open subset
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\subset\overline{W}

containing
0=0_{\overline{W}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
,
finite dimensional normed vector spaces
\mathfrak{C},K
,
an open subset
\mathcal{V}_{K}\subset K
,
and a continuous map

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\,:\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{W},

\displaystyle(k,w)

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(A(k,w),w-B(k,w)\bigr).

Suppose it is close to the identity map on
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
up to the finite dimensional factors
K,\mathfrak{C}
and a contraction in the following sense:

A:\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\mathfrak{C}
maps to the finite dimensional space
\mathfrak{C}
,
and
B:\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\overline{W}
is a contraction near
0\in\overline{W}
parametrized by
k\in\mathcal{V}_{K}
that satisfies (
43
), (
44
).

Then there is a finite dimensional reduction describing the zero set
\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)
, locally, as the zero set of a map between finite dimensional spaces. That is, we have continuous maps

f:\mathcal{V}_{K}\to\mathfrak{C}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003pt\phi:f^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)

such that
\phi
is a homeomorphism to an open subset of
\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)
.
More precisely, Lemma
4.9
constructs a solution map
\sigma:\mathcal{V}_{K}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
, which induces the maps
f(k):=A\bigl(k,\sigma(k)\bigr)
and
\phi(k):=(k,\sigma(k))
.
And with
\delta>0
from (
44
), we have

\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)\cap\bigl\{(k,w)\in\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\,\big|\,\|w\|^{W}<\delta\bigr\}\;=\;\bigl\{\phi(k)\,\big|\,k\in f^{-1}(0)\bigr\}.

If, moreover, the map
\mathcal{G}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
, then the finite dimensional reduction
f:\mathcal{V}_{K}\to\mathfrak{C}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
as well.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.9
, the solutions of
w-B(k,w)=0
with
\|w\|^{W}<\delta
are parametrized by a uniquely determined map
\sigma:\mathcal{V}_{K}\to\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
. Thus we can rewrite the zero set of the original map: For
(k,w)\in\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(k,w)=(0,0),\hskip 10.00002pt\|w\|^{W}<\delta

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(A(k,w),w-B(k,w)\bigr)=(0,0),\hskip 10.00002pt\|w\|^{W}<\delta

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002ptA(k,w)=0,\hskip 10.00002ptw=B(k,w),\hskip 10.00002pt\|w\|^{W}<\delta

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002ptA(k,w)=0,\hskip 10.00002ptw=\sigma(k)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002ptA(k,\sigma(k))=0,\hskip 10.00002pt(k,w)=(k,\sigma(k)).

The resulting map
f(k):=A(k,\sigma(k))
has finite dimensional domain
\mathcal{V}_{K}\subset K
and target
\mathfrak{C}
.
The map
\phi:f^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}
given by
\phi(k):=(k,\sigma(k))
takes values in
\mathcal{G}^{-1}(0)
by construction, is locally surjective as specified above. To check that
\phi
is a homeomorphism to its image, note that
{\rm Pr}_{K}(\phi(k))=k
. This shows that
\phi
is injective with continuous inverse
{\rm Pr}_{K}
.

If, moreover,
\mathcal{G}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular, then both maps
A:\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\mathfrak{C}
and

B:\mathcal{V}_{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W}}\to\overline{W}
are
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular. The latter, combined with Lemma
4.9
, implies
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of
\sigma
. Then
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of

f:\mathcal{V}_{K}\to\mathfrak{C}

k\mapsto A\bigl(k,\sigma(k)\bigr)
follows from the chain rule.
∎

This finishes the proof of Theorem
4.1
, so it remains to finish the proof of Theorem
1.8
.

Corollary 4.12
.

Every adiabatic Fredholm family
\bigl((\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}\to\Omega)_{\epsilon\in\Delta},\ldots\bigr)
as in Definition
2.1
has a finite dimensional reduction describing the union of completed zero sets, locally, as the zero set of a map between finite dimensional spaces. That is, the family induces maps

f:\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathfrak{C}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003pt\phi:f^{-1}(0)\to\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)

defined on neighbourhoods
\Delta_{\sigma}\subset\Delta
of
0
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathfrak{K}
of
0
such that
\phi
is injective and locally surjective in the sense that
for some
\delta_{\sigma}>0
we have

\bigl(\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\bigr)\cap\bigl\{(\epsilon,\gamma)\,\big|\,\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma},\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon},\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma}\bigr\}\;=\;\bigr\{\phi(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\,\big|\,(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\in f^{-1}(0)\bigr\}.

More precisely,
\Delta_{\sigma}
,
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
, and
\delta_{\sigma}
are given by Theorem
4.10
in the process of constructing
solution maps
\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\bigr)_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}
, whose components define

f(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}):=f_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}):={\rm Pr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)
and

\phi(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}):=(\epsilon,\phi_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})):=(\epsilon,{\rm Pr}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)\bigr)
.
This construction also yields an explicit inverse
\phi^{-1}:\operatorname{im}\phi\to f^{-1}(0)
given by

(\epsilon,\gamma)\mapsto(\epsilon,\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma))
.
Finally, it has the following regularity properties:

\bullet

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is fibrewise
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular for some for some
\ell\geq 1
(which is automatic for
\ell=0
and
\ell=1
) as in Definition
2.10
, then the finite dimensional reduction is fibrewise
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular in the sense that the maps

f_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathfrak{C}
are
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular and
\phi_{\epsilon}:f_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\to\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
are homeomorphisms to their images for each
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
.

\bullet

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing as in Definition
2.8
, then its zero sets
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)=\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
agree with the completions and each map
\phi_{\epsilon}:f_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\Gamma
takes values in the
\epsilon
-independent space
\Gamma
.

\bullet

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing and adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
-regular as in Definition
2.13
, then the finite dimensional reduction is adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
in the sense that

f:\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathfrak{C}
is continuous
and
\phi:f^{-1}(0)\to\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\Delta_{\sigma}\times\Gamma
is a homeomorphism onto its image with respect to the relative topology induced by
\Delta_{\sigma}
and
(\Gamma,\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma}_{0})
.

\bullet

If, furthermore, the adiabatic Fredholm family is adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular as in Definition
2.13
for some
\ell\geq 1
, then the finite dimensional reduction is adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
in the sense that

–

the
\ell
-th tangent maps from differentiation in
\mathfrak{K}
(see Definition
2.5
) form a continuous map

\Delta_{\sigma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C},\hskip 10.00002pt(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\mapsto{\rm T}^{\ell}f_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}}),

–

the
\mathfrak{K}
-differentials of order
0\leq k\leq\ell
(see Remark
2.3
) form continuous maps

\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{L}^{k}(\mathfrak{K}^{k},\mathfrak{C}),\hskip 10.00002pt(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mapsto{\rm D}^{k}f_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0}).

Proof.

By Lemma
4.8
, restricted to the subset
\Delta_{\sigma}\subset\Delta_{Q}
from Theorem
4.10
, the completed zero sets
\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\simeq\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{Q}^{-1}(0_{\Delta_{\sigma}})
are naturally identified with the preimage of the "zero section"
0_{\Delta_{\sigma}}:=\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\{(0,0,0)\}
under

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{Q}:\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathfrak{K}\times\bigl(\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\bigr)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{Q}}\{\epsilon\}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\bigl(\overline{W}_{\epsilon}=\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}\bigr),

\displaystyle\bigl(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k},w=(\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\bigr)

\displaystyle\mapsto\bigl(\overline{A}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w),w-\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w)\bigr).

Here
\overline{A}_{\epsilon}:\mathfrak{K}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{C}
maps to the finite dimensional space
\mathfrak{C}
by projection
\overline{A}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)=\mathfrak{c}
.
On the infinite dimensional factor, Theorem
4.10
establishes
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},\cdot)
as contractions whose fixed points define solution maps
\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\bigr)_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}
defined on neighbourhoods
\Delta_{\sigma}\subset\Delta
of
0
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathfrak{K}
of
0

such that for some
\delta_{\sigma}>0
we have

\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\,\big|\,\|\mathfrak{c}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}+\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma},\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{k},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=\mathfrak{c}\bigr\}\;=\;\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))\,\big|\,\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\bigr\}.

We denote the components of the solution maps

\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}=\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}

by

f(\epsilon,\cdot):={\rm Pr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathfrak{C}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptg(\epsilon,\cdot):={\rm Pr}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}\circ\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}

and
note that
g(\epsilon,\cdot):\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
by construction takes values in
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C})\cap\{\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\,\big|\,\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma}\bigr\}
and
solves
\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}\circ g(\epsilon,\cdot)={\rm Id}_{\mathfrak{K}}
.
Then intersecting the above identity of sets with
\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{c},\gamma)\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\,\big|\,\mathfrak{k}=\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),\mathfrak{c}=0\bigr\}

yields

\bigl\{(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)\,\big|\,\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon},\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0\bigr\}\;=\;\bigl\{(\mathfrak{k},0,g(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}))\,\big|\,\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}},f(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})=0\bigr\}.

Next, projecting the sets by

\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\{0\}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
yields

\bigl\{\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}\,\big|\,\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0\bigr\}\;=\;\bigl\{g(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\,\big|\,\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}},f(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})=0\bigr\},

where the
\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}
on the right hand side is uniquely determined by
\gamma=g(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})
.
We keep track of this fact by observing that
g(\epsilon,\cdot):\{\mathfrak{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\,|\,f(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})=0\}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon}
is injective since
\gamma=g(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\;\Rightarrow\;\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma)=\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(g(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}))=\mathfrak{k}
.
Now taking the union over
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
yields the claimed identity

\displaystyle\bigl(\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\bigr)\cap\bigl\{(\epsilon,\gamma)\,\big|\,\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma},\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon},\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\;\bigl\{(\epsilon,\gamma)\,\big|\,\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma},\gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\Gamma},\epsilon},\|\gamma\|^{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}<\delta_{\sigma},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}(\gamma)=0\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\;\bigr\{\phi(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\,\big|\,(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\in f^{-1}(0)\bigr\}

in terms of the maps

\displaystyle f\,:\hskip 10.00002pt\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}

\displaystyle\;\to\;\mathfrak{C},

\displaystyle(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})

\displaystyle\;\mapsto\;{\rm Pr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr),

\displaystyle\phi\,:\;\;\hskip 10.00002ptf^{-1}(0)\;

\displaystyle\;\to\;\textstyle\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}}\{\epsilon\}\times\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0),

\displaystyle(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})

\displaystyle\;\mapsto\;(\epsilon,{\rm Pr}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)\bigr).

Here
\phi
takes values in the union of completed zero sets since each

g(\epsilon,\cdot)={\rm Pr}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}\circ\sigma_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}
takes values in
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
.
It is locally surjective in the sense established above, and it is injective with explicit inverse

\phi^{-1}:\operatorname{im}\phi\to f^{-1}(0)
given by

(\epsilon,\gamma)\mapsto(\epsilon,\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma))
.

This finite dimensional reduction can also be seen as the result of applying Lemma
4.11
for each fixed
\epsilon\in\Delta_{\sigma}
to the map

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{Q}(\epsilon,\cdot,\cdot):\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{W},\epsilon}\to\mathfrak{C}\times\overline{W}_{\epsilon}
,

(\mathfrak{k},w)\mapsto\bigl(\overline{A}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w),w-\overline{B}_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w)\bigr)
.
The resulting finite dimensional reduction is given by
\mathfrak{k}\mapsto A_{\epsilon}\bigl(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)
and
\mathfrak{k}\mapsto(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})
.
Since
A_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k},w=(\mathfrak{c},\gamma))={\rm Pr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(w)=\mathfrak{c}
, this gives rise to
f_{\epsilon}:\mathfrak{k}\mapsto A_{\epsilon}\bigl(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)={\rm Pr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))
. To identify the zero sets, we need to include the identification

\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{Q}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)=\overline{\mathcal{G}}(\epsilon,\cdot)^{-1}(0)\simeq\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
from
Lemmas
4.3
and
4.8
, given by the projection
{\rm Pr}:(\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma),0,\gamma)\mapsto\gamma
. This results, as claimed, in
\phi_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})={\rm Pr}(\mathfrak{k},\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}))={\rm Pr}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)
. The benefit of this identification is that we can now deduce fibrewise regularity from Lemma
4.11
.

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is fibrewise
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular for some
\ell>1
, then each contraction
\overline{B}_{\epsilon}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
by Lemma
4.8
– as is the linear map
\overline{A}_{\epsilon}
.
Thus
f_{\epsilon}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
by Lemma
4.11
.

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing, then its zero sets
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)=\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
agree with the completions by Lemma
4.3
.
This in particular guarantees that each map
\phi_{\epsilon}:f_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)\subset\Gamma
takes values in the
\epsilon
-independent space
\Gamma
.

If the adiabatic Fredholm family is regularizing and adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
-regular, then Theorem
4.10
shows that
(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\mapsto{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})
is a continuous map

\Delta_{\sigma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\bigl({\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{W},\|\cdot\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{0}\bigr)
.
Its target space can be identified with the product
{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{W}={\rm T}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{C}\times\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma})\simeq{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
. In this identification the first factor is the finite dimensional reduction map
{\rm T}^{\ell}f_{\epsilon}={\rm Pr}_{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}}\circ{\rm T}^{\ell}\sigma_{\epsilon}
and the norm is

\|\underline{w}=(\underline{\mathfrak{c}},\underline{\gamma})\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}W}_{0}=\|\underline{\mathfrak{c}}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}}+\|\underline{\gamma}\|^{{\rm T}^{\ell}\Gamma}_{0}
.
Thus continuity of

\Delta_{\sigma}\times{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to{\rm T}^{\ell}\mathfrak{C}
,
(\epsilon,\underline{\mathfrak{k}})\mapsto{\rm T}^{\ell}f_{\epsilon}(\underline{\mathfrak{k}})
follows directly from the adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{\ell}
regularity of the solution maps.

To deduce continuity of the higher differentials, recall from Remark
2.6
that for
0\leq k\leq\ell
the
k
-th tangent map
{\rm T}^{k}f_{\epsilon}
is contained in some components of the
\ell
-th tangent map
{\rm T}^{\ell}f_{\epsilon}
. Moreover, we can recover the
k
-th differential
{\rm D}^{k}f_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})(\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{2^{\ell-1}})
as the last entry of
{\rm T}^{k}f(\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{1},\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{2},0,\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{4},0,\ldots,\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{2^{\ell-1}},0,\ldots,0)
.
Thus – renumbering components
\mathfrak{k}_{i}:=\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}_{2^{i-1}}
– the above continuity of
{\rm T}^{\ell}f_{\epsilon}
also implies continuity of

\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\times\mathfrak{K}^{k}\to\mathfrak{C}
,
(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0},\mathfrak{k}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{k}_{k})\mapsto{\rm D}^{\ell}f_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})
for all
0\leq k\leq\ell
.
Since this map is linear in the last
k
factors, and
\mathfrak{K}
is finite dimensional, this implies the continuity of

\Delta_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{K}}\to\mathcal{L}^{k}(\mathfrak{K}^{k},\mathfrak{C})
,

(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k}_{0})\mapsto{\rm D}^{k}f_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k}_{0})

for all
0\leq k\leq\ell
.

Finally, the adiabatic
\mathcal{C}^{0}
regularity in particular implies that

\phi:f^{-1}(0)\to\Delta_{\sigma}\times\Gamma,(\epsilon,\mathfrak{k})\mapsto\bigl(\epsilon,{\rm Pr}_{\Gamma}\bigl(\sigma_{\epsilon}(\mathfrak{k})\bigr)
is continuous with respect to the relative topology induced by
\Delta_{\sigma}
and
(\Gamma,\|\cdot\|^{\Gamma}_{0})
. It is a homeomorphism onto its image due to continuity of the explicit inverse map

\phi^{-1}:\operatorname{im}\phi\to f^{-1}(0)
,
(\epsilon,\gamma)\mapsto(\epsilon,\overline{\pi}_{\mathfrak{K}}(\gamma))
.
∎
